   22: # validate_strict schema cannot silently diverge.

   24: 
   25: # SQL comparison operators that are safe to interpolate into WHERE clauses.
   26: # Scalar-binding operators: the value is always passed as a bind param (?), so
   27: # no quoting or escaping is required on the value side.  LIKE and NOT LIKE are
   28: # safe for the same reason â€” the pattern is bound, not interpolated.
   29: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_SQL_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('>', '<', '>=', '<=', '!=', '=', 'LIKE', 'NOT LIKE');
   30: 
   31: # List-membership operators whose criterion value is an arrayref.
   32: # Each element is bound as a separate ?, so injection is impossible.
   33: # IN  with an empty list â†’ WHERE 1=0 (no rows match, correct SQL semantics).
   34: # NOT IN with an empty list â†’ no WHERE term (all rows match, correct semantics).
   35: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_LIST_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IN', 'NOT IN');
   36: 
   37: # Nullability operators: no bind parameter.  The hashref value is ignored â€”
   38: # any value (undef, 1, ...) signals intent; only the key selects the operator.
   39: # A bare undef criterion value (col => undef) also generates IS NULL.
   40: Readonly::Hash my %SAFE_NOARG_OPS => map { $_ => 1 } ('IS NULL', 'IS NOT NULL');
   41: 
   42: # Key pushdown (array backend): the most primary keys that are passed to a
   43: # secondary database as one { -in => [...] } criterion.  Each key is a bind
   44: # parameter, and SQLite builds before 3.32 allow at most 999 in a statement.
   45: # Past this, reading the whole secondary is no worse than several queries.
   46: Readonly::Scalar my $MAX_PUSHDOWN_KEYS => 999;
   47: 
   48: # Database::Abstraction's spelling of SQL IN.  It croaks on 'IN'.
   49: Readonly::Scalar my $DA_IN_OP => '-in';
   50: 
   51: # Sentinel for outer-join keys absent from the primary: a single pre-allocated
   52: # [{}] shared across all keys and queries.  The inner loop copies the {} into
   53: # %merged immediately, so the shared hashref is never mutated.
   54: my $_EMPTY_PRIMARY_ROW = [{}];
   55: 
   56: # Valid aggregate functions for aggregate().  Lower-cased at validation time.
   57: Readonly::Hash my %AGGREGATE_FNS => map { $_ => 1 } qw(sum avg min max count count_distinct);
   58: 
   59: # Valid operator names for the mandatory_filters {col, op, val} predicate format.

  121: 
  122: =head1 NAME
  123: 
  124: Database::Join - Read-only combined view across two or more Database::Abstraction objects
  125: 
  126: =head1 VERSION
  127: 
  128: Version 0.011.2
  129: 
  130: =head1 SYNOPSIS
  131: 
  132: B<Basic two-database join>
  133: 
  134:     use Database::Join;
  135: 
  136:     # Step 1: create each component database the normal way
  137:     # See Database::Abstraction
  138:     my $customers = Database::Customers->new(directory => '/data');
  139:     my $loyalty   = Database::Loyalty->new(directory  => '/data');
  140: 
  141:     # Step 2: combine them on the shared key column 'entry'
  142:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  143:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  144:         join_column => 'entry',
  145:     );
  146: 
  147:     # Step 3: query exactly as you would a single Database::Abstraction object
  148:     my $all_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  149:     my $vip_rows  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
  150:     my $one_row   = $join->fetchrow_hashref(entry => 'C001');
  151:     my $total     = $join->count();
  152:     my $col_names = $join->columns();
  153: 
  154: B<Hiding internal columns>
  155: 
  156:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  157:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  158:         join_column    => 'entry',
  159:         remove_columns => [ 'internal_id', 'audit_ts' ],
  160:     );
  161:     # 'internal_id' and 'audit_ts' never appear in results or columns()
  162: 
  163: B<join_map: when the key column has different names in each database>
  164: 
  165:     # $cities  (index 0) has a column called 'statecode' -- matches join_column
  166:     # $stnames (index 1) has a column called 'entry'     -- different name
  167: 
  168:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  169:         databases   => [ $cities,  $stnames ],
  170:         #                index 0   index 1
  171:         join_column => 'statecode',
  172:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },  # index 1 calls its join key 'entry'
  173:     );
  174: 
  175:     # All returned rows use 'statecode'; 'entry' is never exposed
  176:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  177: 
  178: B<filters: permanently restrict a database's visible rows>
  179: 
  180:     # Only show orders placed more than 60 days ago, without repeating
  181:     # the criterion on every query call.
  182:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  183:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
  184:         join_column => 'entry',
  185:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
  186:     );
  187: 
  188:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();                 # all old orders
  189:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');   # old + gold tier
  190: 
  191: B<Inner and outer join types>
  192: 
  193:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
  194:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  195:         join_column => 'entry',
  196:         join_type   => 'inner',   # only keys present in BOTH databases
  197:     );
  198: 
  199:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
  200:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  201:         join_column => 'entry',
  202:         join_type   => 'outer',   # all keys from EITHER database
  203:     );
  204: 
  205: B<Building the view incrementally with add_database>
  206: 
  207:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  208:         databases   => [ $customers ],
  209:         join_column => 'entry',
  210:     );
  211: 
  212:     $join->add_database($loyalty)
  213:          ->add_database($scores, remove_columns => ['raw_score']);
  214: 
  215: B<AUTOLOAD column shortcut>
  216: 
  217:     # Returns the 'name' value for entry 'C001' (scalar context)
  218:     my $name = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
  219: 
  220:     # Returns all 'tier' values (list context)
  221:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
  222: 
  223: B<aggregate(): GROUP BY across the merged view>
  224: 
  225:     # Count and total score per tier, highest total first
  226:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
  227:         by       => ['tier'],
  228:         measures => [
  229:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
  230:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
  231:         ],
  232:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
  233:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
  234:     );
  235:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
  236: 
  237:     # Grand total across the whole view (no 'by' key)
  238:     my $summary = $join->aggregate(
  239:         measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }],
  240:     );
  241:     # => [ { n => 42 } ]
  242: 
  243: B<SQLite join backend for large datasets>
  244: 
  245:     # 'auto' (default): switches to SQLite automatically above the threshold
  246:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  247:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  248:         join_column    => 'entry',
  249:         backend        => 'auto',          # default
  250:         max_array_rows => 50_000,          # use SQLite when combined rows > 50,000
  251:     );
  252: 
  253:     # Always use SQLite -- useful when you know the data is large
  254:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  255:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  256:         join_column => 'entry',
  257:         backend     => 'sqlite',
  258:         tmpdir      => '/fast/nvme/tmp',   # optional: faster temp disk
  259:     );
  260: 
  261:     # Always use the original in-memory path
  262:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  263:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  264:         join_column => 'entry',
  265:         backend     => 'array',
  266:     );
  267: 
  268: =head1 DESCRIPTION
  269: 
  270: C<Database::Join> merges two or more L<Database::Abstraction> objects into a
  271: single logical, read-only view.  Each component database is queried
  272: independently through its own C<Database::Abstraction> interface.  The results
  273: are combined using a shared key column (C<join_column>).
  274: In effect, this means that you can view data from more than one database using
  275: an intuitive, non-SQL interface.
  276: 
  277: Every storage format that C<Database::Abstraction> supports works as a
  278: component database: CSV, PSV, TSV, SQLite, JSON, XML, XLSX, BerkeleyDB, HTML
  279: URL, JSON URL, or any custom subclass.  Component databases may mix formats
  280: within the same join.
  281: 
  282: The module exposes the same read-only API as C<Database::Abstraction>:
  283: C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>, C<count>,
  284: C<columns>, C<schema>, C<updated>, C<set_logger>, and the AUTOLOAD column
  285: shortcut.  Callers do not need to know how many underlying databases are
  286: involved.
  287: 
  288: Think of it as a virtual database table that is assembled on demand from
  289: several real tables, one per component database.
  290: 
  291: B<Join backends>
  292: 
  293: By default (C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'>), C<Database::Join> first checks the
  294: combined source row count.  For small datasets (up to C<max_array_rows>,
  295: default 10,000 rows) it merges entirely in Perl memory.  For larger datasets it
  296: automatically spills source rows into a temporary SQLite database and executes
  297: a single SQL JOIN there, keeping peak RAM to roughly one times the source data
  298: size instead of three.  You can also force either path unconditionally with
  299: C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> or C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>.
  300: 
  301: B<Key pushdown (array backend)>
  302: 
  303: Under a C<left> or C<inner> join, only secondary rows whose key is one of the
  304: primary's can appear in the result.  So once the primary has been read, each
  305: secondary that has no criterion on its join column is asked for just those
  306: keys, as C<< { -in => [ ... ] } >> on its local join column, instead of being
  307: read in full.  Joining a few hundred primary rows to a large secondary table
  308: then costs a few hundred rows, not the whole table.  Pushdown is skipped when:
  309: 
  310: =over 4
  311: 
  312: =item * the join is C<outer>, which needs every secondary key;
  313: 
  314: =item * the primary has no rows, or more than 999 keys (the bind-parameter
  315: limit of older SQLite builds; past that, reading the whole table is no worse);
  316: 
  317: =item * the query already has a criterion on the join column, which then
  318: stands as given;
  319: 
  320: =item * the secondary's C<selectall_arrayref> is not
  321: C<Database::Abstraction>'s own.  A subclass that overrides it, or another
  322: object implementing the interface, may not understand C<-in>.
  323: 
  324: =back
  325: 
  326: If the secondary croaks on the C<-in> query, it is read in full instead.  The
  327: result is the same either way.  Pushdown only narrows what is fetched; it never
  328: counts as the secondary's own criterion, so it can't turn a C<left> join into
  329: an C<inner> one.
  330: 
  331: =head2 Join semantics
  332: 
  333: The C<join_type> parameter controls what happens when a particular key value
  334: exists in some component databases but not all:
  335: 
  336: =over 4
  337: 
  338: =item C<left> (the default)
  339: 
  340: All rows from the I<primary> (first) database are returned.  Columns from
  341: subsequent databases are included where a matching row is found, and simply
  342: absent from the hashref where there is no match.  If you are familiar with
  343: SQL, this is a LEFT OUTER JOIN on the first table.
  344: 
  345: =item C<inner>
  346: 
  347: Only rows whose join-column value is present in I<every> component database
  348: are returned.  This is equivalent to a SQL INNER JOIN.
  349: 
  350: =item C<outer>
  351: 
  352: Every join-column value found in I<any> component database is returned.
  353: Columns from databases that do not have that key value are absent from the
  354: merged row.  This is a FULL OUTER JOIN.
  355: 
  356: =back
  357: 
  358: B<Important override rule:> whenever you pass a query criterion for a column
  359: that belongs to a secondary database, that database automatically acts as an
  360: inner-join partner for that query only -- regardless of C<join_type>.  This
  361: gives WHERE-clause semantics.  For example, if you have a LEFT join but query
  362: C<< tier => 'gold' >> on a secondary database, only rows whose secondary entry
  363: has tier = 'gold' are returned (rows with no secondary entry are excluded, just
  364: as a WHERE clause would exclude them).
  365: 
  366: =head2 Column ownership and routing
  367: 
  368: At construction time, C<Database::Join> calls C<columns()> on each component
  369: database and builds an internal index that maps every column name to the
  370: database that owns it.
  371: 
  372: When you pass criteria to a query method, each key-value pair is automatically
  373: routed to the right database.  You never need to say which database a column
  374: belongs to.
  375: 
  376: The C<join_column> is special: criteria on it are broadcast to I<all>
  377: databases so that each database fetches only the relevant rows before the
  378: in-memory merge.
  379: 
  380: When the same non-join column name exists in more than one database, the
  381: I<last> database in the C<databases> array wins by default: its value
  382: overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.  Use C<collision_prefix> to
  383: preserve both values under distinct names instead.
  384: 
  385: =head1 LIMITATIONS
  386: 
  387: =over 4
  388: 
  389: =item Memory usage (array backend)
  390: 
  391: When C<backend> is C<'array'> (or C<'auto'> and the dataset is small), all
  392: matching rows are fetched into Perl memory.  Key pushdown (see
  393: L</DESCRIPTION>) limits a secondary to the primary's keys under C<left> and
  394: C<inner> joins, but an C<outer> join, or a primary with more than 999 keys,
  395: still reads every secondary row.  Peak RAM is roughly three times
  396: the source data size.  For large datasets use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>, or
  397: leave C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> and set C<max_array_rows> appropriately.
  398: 
  399: =item SQLite backend uses a persistent cache file
  400: 
  401: When the SQLite path is active, a single C<.db> file with a randomly generated
  402: name (chosen by C<File::Temp> to avoid collisions) is created in C<tmpdir> the
  403: first time a query runs on a given C<Database::Join> object.  Source data is
  404: spilled into that file once; subsequent queries against the same object reuse
  405: the file without re-fetching the source data.
  406: 
  407: The cache is automatically invalidated and rebuilt whenever any source
  408: database's C<updated()> timestamp changes (indicating new data), or when
  409: C<add_database()> is called.
  410: 
  411: The file is deleted when the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (typically
  412: when it goes out of scope).  At any given moment no more than one such file
  413: exists per object.  The directory must be writable and have enough free space
  414: for the full source data (once, not per-query).
  415: 
  416: =item No chained builder or raw SQL
  417: 
  418: C<query()> and C<execute()> are not implemented.  Use C<selectall_arrayref>
  419: or C<fetchrow_hashref> instead.
  420: 
  421: =item Single-column equi-join only
  422: 
  423: Joining on more than one column simultaneously, or on expressions, is not
  424: supported.  When the join key has different names in different databases,
  425: use C<join_map> to declare each database's local column name.
  426: 
  427: =item Sort order
  428: 
  429: Results are sorted ascending by C<join_column> by default.  Pass
  430: C<< sort_by => 'colname' >> (or C<< sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC'] >>)
  431: to any query method to override this.  The array path uses string comparison
  432: (C<cmp>); for accurate numeric ordering on large datasets use the SQLite
  433: backend, which sorts natively by type.
  434: 
  435: =item count() on the array backend fetches all rows
  436: 
  437: On the array backend, C<count()> executes the full in-memory join and counts
  438: the resulting rows in Perl; no C<COUNT(*)> is pushed to the component
  439: databases.  On the SQLite backend, C<count()> executes a C<SELECT COUNT(*)>
  440: SQL query against the cached join tables, avoiding a full row transfer.
  441: 
  442: =back
  443: 
  444: =head1 COMMON PITFALLS
  445: 
  446: =over 4
  447: 
  448: =item The join_column must exist in every component database
  449: 
  450: If even one database is missing the join key column, C<new()> (or
  451: C<add_database()>) will C<croak> immediately.  Use C<join_map> when the
  452: column has a different local name in some databases.
  453: 
  454: =item Criteria on a removed column are silently dropped
  455: 
  456: If you call C<remove_column('tier')> and later query
  457: C<< selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold') >>, the criterion is ignored (with a
  458: C<carp> warning) and all rows are returned.  Always pass criteria before
  459: removing columns, or restructure your code to avoid this.
  460: 
  461: =item You cannot remove the join column
  462: 
  463: C<< $join->remove_column($join->join_column) >> will C<croak>.  The join key
  464: is required for the merge to work.
  465: 
  466: =item Left join does not guarantee all columns are populated
  467: 
  468: Under a LEFT join, rows from the primary database that have no matching row
  469: in a secondary database will be returned with I<no keys> from that secondary
  470: database.  Accessing C<< $row->{score} >> on such a row returns C<undef> --
  471: not zero, not an empty string.  Always test C<defined $row->{score}> rather
  472: than just C<$row->{score}> when the secondary match is optional.
  473: 
  474: =item Filters act as inner-join partners
  475: 
  476: Any database that has a C<filters> entry is promoted to an inner-join partner,
  477: regardless of C<join_type>.  A row whose join-key value does not appear in
  478: the filtered database's result is removed from the merged output entirely, not
  479: merely missing its secondary columns.  This is intentional but can be
  480: surprising if you expected LEFT join semantics.
  481: 
  482: =item Criteria-merging replaces scalar filters
  483: 
  484: When both a base filter and a query criterion target the same column, and both
  485: are operator hashrefs (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >>), the operators are combined
  486: (AND semantics).  But if the query criterion is a plain scalar (e.g.
  487: C<< score => 75 >>), it I<replaces> the base filter for that column entirely --
  488: the base filter is ignored for that query.
  489: 
  490: =item AUTOLOAD sees the full merged join when filters or join_map are active
  491: 
  492: When either C<filters> or C<join_map> is in effect, the AUTOLOAD shortcut
  493: (C<< $join->columnname(...) >>) runs the full join query rather than
  494: delegating directly to the owning database.  This is necessary for correctness
  495: but means the result respects all active filters and join-key translations,
  496: which may differ from what the owning database would return on its own.
  497: 
  498: =item Duplicate column names: last database wins (unless collision_prefix is set)
  499: 
  500: When two component databases each have a column called C<notes>, the second
  501: database's value silently overwrites the first in every merged row.  Use
  502: C<collision_prefix => { 1 => 'right' }> to publish the second database's
  503: C<notes> as C<right.notes> so both values survive, or use C<remove_columns>
  504: (or C<remove_column>) to drop the unwanted duplicate entirely.
  505: 
  506: =item Mutating the filters hashref after construction has no effect
  507: 
  508: C<Database::Join> deep-copies the C<filters> hashref (and any C<filter>
  509: passed to C<add_database>) at the moment of construction.  The original hashref
  510: you passed in is never stored.  If you later modify it -- for example, to
  511: tighten or loosen a filter criterion -- the joined view is I<not> affected.
  512: Construct a new C<Database::Join> object, or use a component
  513: C<Database::Abstraction> that supports dynamic filter modification.
  514: 
  515: =item auto mode may always use the array path for some DAs
  516: 
  517: C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> counts rows cheaply only when each component database
  518: either implements C<dbi_source()> (SQLite-backed) or directly defines a
  519: C<count()> method in its own package.  A DA that merely I<inherits> C<count()>
  520: from C<Database::Abstraction> is treated as uncountable, because the parent
  521: class C<count()> expects a key argument and behaves differently from a
  522: "return total row count" function.  In that case C<Database::Join>
  523: conservatively uses the array path for the whole query, even if the dataset is
  524: large.  To opt in to the SQLite path for such a DA, either add your own
  525: C<count()> override that returns the total row count, implement C<dbi_source()>,
  526: or use C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> unconditionally.
  527: 
  528: =item dbi_source() ATTACH is unconditional - query-time criteria go into WHERE
  529: 
  530: When a component database implements C<dbi_source()>, C<Database::Join> always
  531: uses the zero-copy ATTACH path, I<even when the current query includes criteria
  532: for columns in that database>.  The criteria are translated into parameterised
  533: SQL C<WHERE> clauses applied against the ATTACHed table; no row-level copy is
  534: performed.  (Prior to 0.005.0 the presence of any query-time criteria would
  535: force a spill; that restriction has been removed.)
  536: 
  537: =item Broadcast join-column criterion does not force secondaries into inner-join
  538: 
  539: When a caller passes a join-column criterion (e.g. C<entry =E<gt> 'k1'>),
  540: C<Database::Join> broadcasts it to all component databases so each DA can
  541: filter its fetch to the requested key.  Prior to 0.006.0 this broadcast was
  542: incorrectly counted as "having criteria" for secondary databases, causing
  543: C<left> and C<outer> joins to silently behave as C<inner> joins when a
  544: join-column criterion was present.  The fix: only non-join-column criteria
  545: (e.g. column filters from the caller or base C<filters =E<gt> {...}>) promote
  546: a secondary to inner-join status.  The broadcast itself is now a transparent
  547: key-range selector that does not affect join semantics.
  548: 
  549: =item LIKE and NOT LIKE work on the SQLite path; other pattern operators do not yet
  550: 
  551: C<LIKE>, C<NOT LIKE>, C<IN>, and C<NOT IN> are fully supported on the SQLite
  552: backend.  C<LIKE>/C<NOT LIKE> take a scalar pattern; C<IN>/C<NOT IN> take an
  553: arrayref of values.  All are injection-safe because values are passed as bind
  554: parameters, never interpolated.
  555: 
  556:     # LIKE
  557:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(name => { LIKE => 'A%' });
  558: 
  559:     # IN
  560:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { IN => ['gold', 'silver'] });
  561: 
  562:     # NOT IN
  563:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => { 'NOT IN' => ['bronze'] });
  564: 
  565: C<IN> with an empty arrayref matches no rows (SQL semantics: C<IN ()> is
  566: always false).  C<NOT IN> with an empty arrayref matches all rows (no
  567: constraint added).
  568: 
  569: C<IS NULL> and C<IS NOT NULL> are supported on the SQLite backend using an
  570: explicit operator hashref:
  571: 
  572:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NULL'     => undef });
  573:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { 'IS NOT NULL' => 1    });
  574: 
  575: The hashref value is ignored; only the key selects the operator.  A bare
  576: C<undef> criterion value (C<< score => undef >>) also generates C<IS NULL>
  577: on the SQLite path.  Note: on the in-memory array path, the component DA
  578: may treat an C<undef> criterion value as C<< no filter >> rather than
  579: C<IS NULL>, so use the explicit hashref form for consistent behaviour
  580: across backends.
  581: 
  582: Any operator not in the supported set is skipped on the SQLite path with a
  583: C<carp> warning.  The array path forwards all operators to the component DA
  584: unchanged, which may or may not honour them.
  585: 
  586: =item Temp file directory must be writable and have free space
  587: 
  588: The SQLite path creates one temporary C<.db> file per C<Database::Join> object
  589: in C<tmpdir> (default: C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()>, usually C</tmp> on Unix).
  590: The filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp> -- you cannot predict it,
  591: only the directory is under your control.  The file is created on the first
  592: query and kept alive until the object is destroyed; it is not re-created on
  593: every query call.  If the directory is not writable, or the filesystem is full,
  594: the call will C<croak> with C<error_sqlite_connect>.  Check permissions and
  595: free space if you see that error.
  596: 
  597: =item C<join =E<gt>> criteria are not supported
  598: 
  599: C<Database::Abstraction> accepts a C<join =E<gt> { table =E<gt> ..., on =E<gt> ... }>
  600: key in its criteria hashrefs to express an SQL JOIN within a single table.
  601: C<Database::Join> cannot route this to a component DA meaningfully: the merged
  602: view has no concept of a single underlying table.  Passing C<join =E<gt>> to any
  603: query method will C<croak> with C<error_join_criterion>.
  604: 
  605: B<Fix:> Model the joined table as a separate C<Database::Abstraction> object and
  606: add it to the C<databases =E<gt> []> list.  Use C<join_column> or C<join_map> to
  607: specify the shared key.
  608: 
  609: =item aggregate(): omitting C<by> gives a grand total, not an error
  610: 
  611: Calling C<aggregate()> without a C<by> key (or with C<< by => [] >>) does
  612: I<not> croak.  It returns a single-element arrayref containing one row with
  613: all the computed measure aliases.  This is a grand total over the entire view
  614: (possibly filtered by any extra criteria you pass).
  615: 
  616:     my $total = $join->aggregate(measures => [{ fn => 'count', as => 'n' }]);
  617:     # $total->[0]{n} is the total row count
  618: 
  619: If you expected multiple rows and got one, you probably forgot the C<by> key.
  620: 
  621: =item aggregate(): non-numeric values behave differently across backends
  622: 
  623: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), any non-numeric value in a
  624: column targeted by C<sum> or C<avg> is I<skipped> (not counted, not coerced),
  625: and a C<carp> warning C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric> is emitted.  On the SQLite
  626: backend, non-numeric values are silently coerced to C<0.0> via SQL C<CAST>.
  627: The two backends therefore produce different numeric totals when the data
  628: contains non-numeric values.  Clean your data first if you need consistent
  629: results across backends, or choose a backend explicitly.
  630: 
  631: =item aggregate(): C<BETWEEN> is not supported in C<having>
  632: 
  633: The C<having> hashref uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>,
  634: which does not include C<BETWEEN>.  Write a range as two explicit constraints:
  635: 
  636:     # Correct
  637:     having => { total => { '>=' => 10, '<=' => 50 } }
  638: 
  639:     # Wrong -- will croak with error_aggregate_having_operator
  640:     having => { total => { BETWEEN => [10, 50] } }
  641: 
  642: =item aggregate(): C<count> without C<col> counts all rows, not distinct non-NULL values
  643: 
  644: A measure with C<< fn => 'count' >> and no C<col> key emits C<COUNT(*)> on the
  645: SQLite backend (or counts every row in Perl).  This counts rows that have
  646: C<undef>/NULL in other columns too.  To count only rows where a specific
  647: column is non-NULL, or to count distinct values, use C<count_distinct>:
  648: 
  649:     # Count rows with a non-NULL 'score' (one way -- use a pre-filter)
  650:     measures => [{ col => 'score', fn => 'count_distinct', as => 'distinct_scores' }]
  651: 
  652: =item aggregate(): the result is never cached
  653: 
  654: Unlike C<selectall_arrayref> (which benefits from the SQLite file cache on
  655: repeated identical calls), C<aggregate()> always re-executes.  If your
  656: aggregation is expensive and the data changes infrequently, cache the result
  657: yourself using C<updated()> as a staleness signal.
  658: 
  659: =item distinct => 1 without columns returns full rows, not a column projection
  660: 
  661: C<< distinct => 1 >> alone deduplicates on I<all> columns of the merged row.
  662: If you want a distinct list of values for a single column (e.g. all unique
  663: tiers), you B<must> supply C<< columns => ['tier'] >> as well.  Without
  664: C<columns>, every column participates in the key used to detect duplicates,
  665: which means two rows that differ in any column (e.g. C<entry>) are never
  666: considered duplicates, and the result will be the same as without C<distinct>.
  667: 
  668: =item distinct => 1 with columns projects rows to those columns
  669: 
  670: When C<columns> is supplied alongside C<distinct>, each returned row contains
  671: I<only> the named columns -- all other columns are dropped.  The projection
  672: happens before deduplication, so the result set may be smaller than the full
  673: merged row count.  If you need the full row I<and> want to know unique values,
  674: run two separate queries or post-process in Perl.
  675: 
  676: =item mandatory_filters must never come from user-supplied input
  677: 
  678: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> form an enforcement boundary.  They must
  679: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
  680: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
  681: HTTP headers, or other user-controlled input.  A hostile user who can control
  682: a mandatory_filters predicate can widen it to C<{ col => 'status', op => 'ne',
  683: val => 'NONEXISTENT' }> and receive all rows regardless of status.
  684: 
  685: =item mandatory_filters uses string comparison on the array path
  686: 
  687: On the array backend, all C<mandatory_filters> comparisons (including C<lt>,
  688: C<lte>, C<gt>, C<gte>) are I<lexicographic string comparisons>, not numeric.
  689: C<< { col => 'score', op => 'gt', val => 9 } >> will match a score of C<'10'>
  690: only if C<'10' gt '9'> is true -- which it is not (C<'1' lt '9'>).  Use the
  691: SQLite backend (C<< backend => 'sqlite' >>) when numeric comparisons are needed,
  692: as SQLite uses the column's declared type for ordering.
  693: 
  694: =back
  695: 
  696: =head1 METHODS
  697: 
  698: =head2 new
  699: 
  700: =head3 SYNOPSIS
  701: 
  702:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  703:         databases         => [ $db1, $db2 ],
  704:         join_column       => 'entry',
  705:         join_type         => 'left',
  706:         join_map          => { 1 => 'local_col' },
  707:         filters           => { 1 => { score => { '>' => 60 } } },
  708:         base_criteria     => { active => 1 },
  709:         mandatory_filters => [{ col => 'region', op => 'eq', val => 'West' }],
  710:         collision_prefix  => { 1 => 'right' },
  711:         remove_columns    => [ 'email', 'internal_id' ],
  712:         backend           => 'auto',        # 'auto' | 'sqlite' | 'array'
  713:         max_array_rows    => 10_000,        # threshold for 'auto' mode
  714:         tmpdir            => '/tmp',        # directory for temp SQLite file
  715:         logger            => $log,
  716:         i18n              => $locale,
  717:     );
  718: 
  719: =head3 DESCRIPTION
  720: 
  721: Constructs and returns a new C<Database::Join> object.
  722: 
  723: Each element of C<databases> must be an already-instantiated subclass of
  724: C<Database::Abstraction>.  The constructor calls C<columns()> on every
  725: database to build an internal column-routing table and verifies that
  726: C<join_column> (or its local alias from C<join_map>) is present in each one.
  727: 
  728: Columns listed in C<remove_columns> are hidden immediately: they do not appear
  729: in C<columns()>, C<schema()>, or any returned row hashref.  This is equivalent
  730: to calling C<remove_column> once per name after construction.
  731: 
  732: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
  733: 
  734: =head4 Input
  735: 
  736:     databases      => { type => 'arrayref', required => 1 }
  737:                       # One or more Database::Abstraction subclass objects.
  738:                       #
  739:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   non-empty arrayref of blessed DA subclasses.
  740:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: scalar, hashref, or absent => croak.
  741:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA size:   minimum 1 element; no documented upper bound.
  742:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA elem:   each element must pass isa('Database::Abstraction').
  743: 
  744:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'entry' }
  745:                       # The column name shared by all databases (the join key).
  746:                       #
  747:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-empty string present in every component DA.
  748:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: column absent from any DA => croak join_col_missing.
  749:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        empty string '' is treated as a column name and
  750:                       #                       will croak if (as expected) it is absent from every DA.
  751:                       # DOMAIN -- NOTE:       matching is case-sensitive and exact.
  752: 
  753:     join_type      => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'left',
  754:                         enum => ['inner', 'left', 'outer'] }
  755:                       # Controls which keys appear in the result when not all
  756:                       # databases share the same key values.
  757:                       #
  758:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   exactly 'inner', 'left', or 'outer'.
  759:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string including 'INNER', 'LEFT',
  760:                       #                       'OUTER' (enum check is case-sensitive), 'cross',
  761:                       #                       or '' => croak from validate_strict.
  762: 
  763:     join_map       => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  764:                       # Zero-based database index => local column name.
  765:                       # See the join_map section for full details.
  766:                       #
  767:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref values must be plain strings.
  768:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: reference value (hashref, arrayref, coderef, etc.)
  769:                       #                       => croak; the guard prevents heap-address leakage.
  770:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        out-of-range keys (beyond the databases array) are
  771:                       #                       silently ignored.
  772: 
  773:     filters        => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  774:                       # Zero-based database index => criteria hashref.
  775:                       # Permanent row restrictions on individual databases.
  776:                       # See the filters section for full details.
  777: 
  778:     base_criteria  => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
  779:                       # Column name => value hashref.
  780:                       # Permanent criteria applied to every query, specified by
  781:                       # column name rather than database index.  Each key must
  782:                       # be a column that appears in the merged view (or the
  783:                       # join_column); each value is a plain scalar or an
  784:                       # operator hashref in the same format as
  785:                       # selectall_arrayref accepts.  Criteria are automatically
  786:                       # routed to the database that owns each column (same
  787:                       # routing used by selectall_arrayref).
  788:                       # Equivalent to filters but more convenient when you know
  789:                       # the column name but not which database index owns it.
  790:                       # When a column appears in both base_criteria and filters,
  791:                       # the filters entry takes precedence.
  792:                       # See the base_criteria section for full details.
  793:                       #
  794:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of column-name => scalar or
  795:                       #                       operator-hashref pairs.  Unknown column
  796:                       #                       names emit warn_unknown_column (carp)
  797:                       #                       and are silently dropped.
  798:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-hashref value => croak from
  799:                       #                       validate_strict.
  800:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        {} empty hashref is a safe no-op.
  801: 
  802:     collision_prefix => { type => 'hashref', optional => 1 }
  803:                       # Zero-based database index (>0) => prefix string.
  804:                       # When a secondary database has a column that collides
  805:                       # with a column already present in the merged view, the
  806:                       # secondary column is published as "$prefix.$col" instead
  807:                       # of silently overwriting the earlier value.
  808:                       # Index 0 entries are silently ignored.
  809:                       # Omitting this parameter preserves the original
  810:                       # last-database-wins behaviour.
  811:                       # See the collision_prefix section for full details.
  812:                       #
  813:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   absent or {} => last-database-wins (no change).
  814:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   { N => 'prefix' } where N > 0 => colliding
  815:                       #                       columns from DB[N] published as "$prefix.$col";
  816:                       #                       non-colliding columns from the same DB added plain.
  817:                       # DOMAIN -- EP note:    index-0 entries are silently ignored.
  818:                       # DOMAIN -- Invariant:  join_column is never prefixed regardless of
  819:                       #                       collision_prefix configuration.
  820: 
  821:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  822:                       # Column names to hide from the merged view.
  823:                       #
  824:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of any strings; non-existent columns
  825:                       #                       are silently ignored (idempotent).
  826:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak remove_join_col.
  827:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        [] empty arrayref is a safe no-op.
  828: 
  829:     backend        => { type => 'string',   optional => 1, default => 'auto',
  830:                         enum => ['array', 'sqlite', 'auto'] }
  831:                       # Controls which join strategy is used.
  832:                       #   'auto'   -- (default) use 'array' when combined source row count
  833:                       #              <= max_array_rows, 'sqlite' otherwise.
  834:                       #   'sqlite' -- always spill to a temporary SQLite database.
  835:                       #   'array'  -- always use the in-memory merge path.
  836:                       #
  837:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto' (case-sensitive).
  838:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other string => croak error_invalid_backend.
  839:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    'auto'.
  840: 
  841:     max_array_rows => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 10_000 }
  842:                       # Row-count threshold for 'auto' mode.  When the combined
  843:                       # source row count exceeds this value, the SQLite path is used.
  844:                       # Ignored when backend is 'array' or 'sqlite'.
  845:                       #
  846:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any non-negative integer.
  847:                       # DOMAIN -- BVA:        0 means always use SQLite (all counts exceed 0).
  848:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    10,000.
  849: 
  850:     tmpdir         => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  851:                       # Directory for the per-call temporary SQLite database file.
  852:                       # The file is created securely by File::Temp and removed when
  853:                       # the query completes.  Ignored when backend is 'array'.
  854:                       #
  855:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any writable directory path string.
  856:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  uses File::Spec->tmpdir() (system temp dir).
  857: 
  858:     parallel       => { type => 'integer',  optional => 1, default => 0 }
  859:                       # When set to 1 and the join has more than 2 databases (primary +
  860:                       # 2 or more secondaries), secondary DA fetches are issued in
  861:                       # parallel Perl threads.  Requires the 'threads' module; falls
  862:                       # back to sequential with a carp warning when unavailable.
  863:                       # Has no effect on the SQLite backend (which uses a single SQL
  864:                       # JOIN).  DBI-backed DAs are not thread-safe by default; only
  865:                       # enable this for in-memory or otherwise thread-safe DA backends.
  866:                       #
  867:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   0 (sequential, default) or 1 (parallel).
  868:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: any other integer is treated as truthy/falsy.
  869:                       # DOMAIN -- Default:    0.
  870: 
  871:     autoload_pk     => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
  872:                       # The column name used as the positional-argument key when
  873:                       # AUTOLOAD performs a full join query (i.e. when join_map or
  874:                       # filters is active).  Calling $join->col('value') with a bare
  875:                       # scalar maps that scalar to { autoload_pk => 'value' } before
  876:                       # routing through the merged view.
  877:                       #
  878:                       # Defaults to join_column when omitted.  Override when the
  879:                       # primary DA's natural lookup key differs from join_column -
  880:                       # for example, when join_column is 'statecode' but you want to
  881:                       # look up by city name ('entry').
  882:                       #
  883:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that is a visible column in
  884:                       #                       the merged view (checked at call time by
  885:                       #                       _partition_criteria; unknown names are
  886:                       #                       carp'd and dropped).
  887:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  defaults to join_column.
  888: 
  889:     mandatory_filters => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
  890:                       # Arrayref of {col, op, val} predicate hashrefs.
  891:                       # These filters are applied to every query result regardless
  892:                       # of what criteria the caller supplies.  They cannot be
  893:                       # overridden, suppressed, or circumvented at query time.
  894:                       # Each predicate must supply:
  895:                       #   col -- a column name present in the merged view
  896:                       #   op  -- one of: eq, ne, lt, lte, gt, gte, like, contains
  897:                       #   val -- the comparison value (plain scalar)
  898:                       # On the array backend: applied as a Perl-side grep after the
  899:                       # in-memory merge, before sort/limit/offset.
  900:                       # On the SQLite backend: injected as parameterised WHERE clauses
  901:                       # into the per-call SQL query.
  902:                       #
  903:                       # SECURITY: Never set this from URL parameters or any
  904:                       # user-supplied value.  Predicates must come from the
  905:                       # server-side session or policy store only.
  906:                       #
  907:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   [] empty arrayref => no constraint (safe no-op).
  908:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of {col, op, val} hashrefs where
  909:                       #                       col is in the merged view and op is supported.
  910:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: element is not a hashref, or is missing
  911:                       #                       col/op/val => croak error_mandatory_filter_ref.
  912:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: col not in merged view => croak error_mandatory_filter_col.
  913:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: op not in supported set => croak error_mandatory_filter_op.
  914:                       # DOMAIN -- ops:        eq (string eq), ne (string ne),
  915:                       #                       lt/lte/gt/gte (string comparison),
  916:                       #                       like (SQL LIKE pattern with % and _),
  917:                       #                       contains (substring match, equivalent to LIKE %val%).
  918: 
  919:     logger         => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  920:                       # Logger object propagated to all component databases.
  921: 
  922:     i18n           => { type => 'object',   optional => 1 }
  923:                       # Localisation object with a translate($key, @args) method.
  924: 
  925: =head4 Output
  926: 
  927:     A blessed Database::Join object.
  928: 
  929: =head3 EXAMPLE
  930: 
  931:     # Customers database: entry | name | email
  932:     # Loyalty   database: entry | tier | points
  933: 
  934:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
  935:         databases      => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
  936:         join_column    => 'entry',
  937:         join_type      => 'inner',            # only customers who also have loyalty records
  938:         remove_columns => [ 'email' ],        # hide PII from query results
  939:         filters        => { 1 => { points => { '>' => 0 } } }, # ignore zero-point records
  940:     );
  941: 
  942:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
  943:     # Each row: { entry => ..., name => ..., tier => ..., points => ... }
  944:     # 'email' is absent. Zero-point loyalty records are excluded.
  945: 
  946: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
  947: 
  948:     validate all parameters with validate_strict
  949:     croak if databases is empty
  950:     croak if any element of databases is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
  951:     bless the object with all fields initialised
  952:     call _build_col_index to map every column to its owning database
  953:         and verify join_column presence in each database
  954:     if base_criteria given:
  955:         partition base_criteria by column ownership into per-db slices
  956:         for each database with a non-empty slice:
  957:             merge the slice into _filters[i]
  958:             (explicit filters win on plain-scalar conflicts)
  959:     if mandatory_filters given:
  960:         for each predicate in mandatory_filters:
  961:             croak error_mandatory_filter_ref if predicate is not a {col, op, val} hashref
  962:             croak error_mandatory_filter_col if col is not in the merged view
  963:             croak error_mandatory_filter_op  if op is not in the supported set
  964:         store shallow copies of all validated predicates in _mandatory_filters
  965:     for each column in remove_columns: call remove_column
  966:     return the new object
  967: 
  968: =head3 MESSAGES
  969: 
  970:     error_no_databases           -- databases arrayref was empty
  971:     error_invalid_db             -- an element of databases is not a D::A subclass
  972:     error_join_col_missing       -- join_column (or its join_map alias) not found in a database
  973:     error_invalid_backend        -- backend value is not 'array', 'sqlite', or 'auto'
  974:     error_sqlite_connect         -- temporary SQLite database could not be created (backend='sqlite'/'auto')
  975:     error_mandatory_filter_ref   -- a mandatory_filters element is not a hashref with col, op, and val keys
  976:     error_mandatory_filter_col   -- a mandatory_filters element names a column not in the merged view
  977:     error_mandatory_filter_op    -- a mandatory_filters element uses an unsupported op value
  978:     warn_schema_type_mismatch    -- (carp) a shared column has different types across databases;
  979:                                     use collision_prefix to preserve both values
  980: 
  981: =cut
  982:
