 1139: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1140: # Public API (mirrors Database::Abstraction)
 1141: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
 1142: 
 1143: =head2 join_map - joining on differently-named columns
 1144: 
 1145: By default every component database must have a column whose name matches
 1146: C<join_column>.  If a database uses a different local name for the join key,
 1147: declare the mapping with C<join_map>.
 1148: 
 1149: C<join_map> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1150: database in the C<databases> array (0 = first, 1 = second, and so on).  Each
 1151: B<value> is the name that B<that particular database> uses for the join key.
 1152: 
 1153: Databases not listed in C<join_map> are assumed to already have a column
 1154: named C<join_column> and need no entry.
 1155: 
 1156: Throughout the merged view the join key is I<always> referred to by the name
 1157: given in C<join_column>.  The local alias is never exposed in returned rows,
 1158: in C<columns()>, or in C<schema()>.
 1159: 
 1160: B<When do you need join_map?>
 1161: 
 1162: You need C<join_map> when you have two tables like:
 1163: 
 1164:     cities table  : entry (the city name) | statecode
 1165:     stnames table : entry (the state code) | state
 1166: 
 1167: Here you want to join cities.statecode to stnames.entry.  You choose
 1168: C<< join_column => 'statecode' >> as the canonical name, but stnames calls
 1169: that same concept C<entry>, so you declare:
 1170: 
 1171:     join_map => { 1 => 'entry' }  # stnames (index 1) calls it 'entry'
 1172: 
 1173: B<Example>
 1174: 
 1175:     #                        index 0     index 1
 1176:     my @databases = (       $cities,    $stnames  );
 1177:     #  join key column:    'statecode'  'entry'
 1178:     #  join_column:        'statecode' (chosen canonical name)
 1179:     #  stnames differs, so declare the alias:
 1180: 
 1181:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1182:         databases   => \@databases,
 1183:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1184:         join_map    => { 1 => 'entry' },
 1185:     );
 1186: 
 1187:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1188:     # Each $row has keys: entry (city), statecode, state
 1189:     # 'entry' from stnames is never exposed directly.
 1190: 
 1191:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(statecode => 'CA');
 1192: 
 1193: B<Using add_database instead>
 1194: 
 1195: If you build the join incrementally with C<add_database>, pass
 1196: C<join_column> directly to that call instead of using C<join_map>:
 1197: 
 1198:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1199:         databases   => [ $cities ],
 1200:         join_column => 'statecode',
 1201:     );
 1202:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'entry');
 1203: 
 1204: This is exactly equivalent to the C<join_map> form above.
 1205: 
 1206: =head2 filters - permanent per-database row filters
 1207: 
 1208: C<filters> lets you restrict a component database to a subset of its rows
 1209: permanently, without repeating the criterion on every query call.
 1210: 
 1211: Think of it as telling the join: "whenever you query this database, always
 1212: add these extra conditions".  Callers never need to specify the restriction
 1213: themselves and can never accidentally omit it.
 1214: 
 1215: C<filters> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based position> of a
 1216: database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).  Each
 1217: B<value> is a criteria hashref in the same format as C<selectall_arrayref>
 1218: accepts.
 1219: 
 1220: B<Key-set semantics>
 1221: 
 1222: A filtered database always acts as an inner-join partner, regardless of the
 1223: C<join_type> setting.  Any join-key value that does not pass the filter is
 1224: excluded from the merged output entirely -- not just missing its secondary
 1225: columns.  This ensures the filter genuinely restricts the view rather than
 1226: simply hiding a few fields.
 1227: 
 1228: B<Criteria merging>
 1229: 
 1230: When a query call also passes a criterion for a column that already has a base
 1231: filter, the two constraints are combined:
 1232: 
 1233: =over 4
 1234: 
 1235: =item *
 1236: 
 1237: When both the base filter value and the query criterion are operator hashrefs
 1238: (e.g. C<< { '>' => 60 } >> and C<< { '<' => 365 } >>), their operators are
 1239: merged: I<both> constraints apply simultaneously (AND semantics).
 1240: 
 1241: =item *
 1242: 
 1243: When either value is a plain scalar, or the operators conflict, the
 1244: query-time criterion wins and the base filter for that column is ignored for
 1245: that one call.
 1246: 
 1247: =back
 1248: 
 1249: B<Example -- only show orders placed more than 60 days ago>
 1250: 
 1251:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1252:         databases   => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1253:         join_column => 'entry',
 1254:         filters     => { 1 => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } } },
 1255:     );
 1256: 
 1257:     # Every query automatically sees only old orders
 1258:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1259: 
 1260:     # Additional criteria layer on top -- gold tier AND old order
 1261:     my $vip  = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1262: 
 1263:     # Range intersection: age_days > 60 AND age_days < 365
 1264:     my $mid  = $join->selectall_arrayref(age_days => { '<' => 365 });
 1265: 
 1266: When using C<add_database>, pass C<filter> (singular) to set the base
 1267: criteria for the new database:
 1268: 
 1269:     $join->add_database($orders, filter => { age_days => { '>' => 60 } });
 1270: 
 1271: =head2 base_criteria - permanent view-level row filters by column name
 1272: 
 1273: C<base_criteria> is a convenience alternative to C<filters> for callers who
 1274: know the column names they want to restrict but prefer not to track database
 1275: indices.
 1276: 
 1277:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1278:         databases     => [ $customers, $orders ],
 1279:         join_column   => 'entry',
 1280:         base_criteria => { active => 1, deleted_at => undef },
 1281:     );
 1282: 
 1283:     # Every query automatically sees only active, non-deleted rows.
 1284:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1285: 
 1286: At construction time C<base_criteria> is partitioned by column ownership
 1287: using the same routing logic as C<selectall_arrayref>.  Each criterion is
 1288: sent to the database that owns that column and stored as a permanent base
 1289: filter (equivalent to the corresponding C<filters> entry).
 1290: 
 1291: B<Unknown columns> emit a C<warn_unknown_column> carp and are silently
 1292: dropped, just as they would be in a query call.
 1293: 
 1294: B<Key-set semantics> are identical to C<filters>: any database that receives
 1295: a C<base_criteria> slice acts as an inner-join partner regardless of
 1296: C<join_type>.
 1297: 
 1298: B<Precedence>: when a column appears in both C<base_criteria> and C<filters>,
 1299: the C<filters> entry wins on plain-scalar conflicts; operator-hashref values
 1300: are combined with AND semantics.
 1301: 
 1302: B<Use cases>
 1303: 
 1304: =over 4
 1305: 
 1306: =item *
 1307: 
 1308: Row-level security: C<< base_criteria => { tenant_id => $tid } >>
 1309: 
 1310: =item *
 1311: 
 1312: Soft-delete filtering: C<< base_criteria => { deleted_at => undef } >>
 1313: 
 1314: =item *
 1315: 
 1316: Status gates: C<< base_criteria => { active => 1 } >>
 1317: 
 1318: =back
 1319: 
 1320: =head2 mandatory_filters - row-level security predicates that cannot be bypassed
 1321: 
 1322: C<mandatory_filters> is an arrayref of C<{col, op, val}> predicate hashrefs.
 1323: Every predicate is applied to the merged result of every query, regardless of
 1324: what criteria the caller passes.  Unlike C<filters> and C<base_criteria> (which
 1325: are per-database constraints applied at fetch time), C<mandatory_filters>
 1326: operates on the fully-merged row set, ensuring the constraint applies uniformly
 1327: even when the same logical value is spread across multiple databases.
 1328: 
 1329:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1330:         databases         => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 1331:         join_column       => 'entry',
 1332:         mandatory_filters => [
 1333:             { col => 'region',  op => 'eq',  val => 'West' },
 1334:             { col => 'tier',    op => 'ne',  val => 'banned' },
 1335:             { col => 'balance', op => 'gte', val => 0        },
 1336:         ],
 1337:     );
 1338: 
 1339: Each predicate hashref must supply exactly these three keys:
 1340: 
 1341: =over 4
 1342: 
 1343: =item C<col>
 1344: 
 1345: The column name in the merged view (as returned by C<columns()>).  Must be
 1346: present at construction time; columns added later via C<add_database()> are not
 1347: retroactively covered by predicates already in C<_mandatory_filters>.
 1348: 
 1349: =item C<op>
 1350: 
 1351: The comparison operator.  Supported values:
 1352: 
 1353:     eq       -- string equality      ($row->{col} eq $val)
 1354:     ne       -- string inequality    ($row->{col} ne $val)
 1355:     lt       -- string less-than     ($row->{col} lt $val)
 1356:     lte      -- string <=            ($row->{col} le $val)
 1357:     gt       -- string greater-than  ($row->{col} gt $val)
 1358:     gte      -- string >=            ($row->{col} ge $val)
 1359:     like     -- SQL LIKE pattern     (% = any sequence, _ = any one character)
 1360:     contains -- substring match      (row value must contain val as a substring)
 1361: 
 1362: Comparisons are lexicographic (string-based) on the array path; on the SQLite
 1363: path they follow SQLite's default collation for the column type.  Use the
 1364: SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) when numeric ordering matters.
 1365: 
 1366: =item C<val>
 1367: 
 1368: The right-hand side of the comparison.  Must be a plain scalar.
 1369: 
 1370: =back
 1371: 
 1372: B<Supported use cases>
 1373: 
 1374: =over 4
 1375: 
 1376: =item *
 1377: 
 1378: Row-level security: restrict the view to rows the current session is authorised
 1379: to see: C<< { col => 'tenant_id', op => 'eq', val => $session_tenant } >>
 1380: 
 1381: =item *
 1382: 
 1383: Data classification gates: hide rows marked with a sensitivity label:
 1384: C<< { col => 'classification', op => 'ne', val => 'restricted' } >>
 1385: 
 1386: =item *
 1387: 
 1388: Substring/pattern guards:
 1389: C<< { col => 'email', op => 'contains', val => '@example.com' } >>
 1390: or C<< { col => 'code', op => 'like', val => 'PROD_%' } >>
 1391: 
 1392: =back
 1393: 
 1394: B<SECURITY: C<mandatory_filters> must never be set from user-supplied values>
 1395: 
 1396: The predicates in C<mandatory_filters> are an enforcement boundary.  They must
 1397: come from a trusted server-side source (a session store, a policy table, or
 1398: hard-coded application configuration) -- never from URL parameters, form fields,
 1399: HTTP headers, or any other user-controlled input.  Setting this parameter from
 1400: user-supplied data would allow an attacker to widen or replace the constraints
 1401: and access rows they are not permitted to see.
 1402: 
 1403: B<Array vs. SQLite backend>
 1404: 
 1405: On the B<array backend>, C<mandatory_filters> is applied as a Perl-side C<grep>
 1406: after the in-memory merge, immediately before C<sort_by>/C<limit>/C<offset>.
 1407: A row that does not satisfy every predicate is silently dropped.
 1408: 
 1409: On the B<SQLite backend>, each predicate is translated into a parameterised
 1410: C<WHERE> clause and injected into the per-call SQL query.  Values are always
 1411: passed via DBI bind parameters, never by string interpolation.
 1412: 
 1413: Both backends produce identical filtered results.
 1414: 
 1415: =head2 distinct - deduplicate merged rows
 1416: 
 1417: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> to C<selectall_arrayref> or C<selectall_array> to
 1418: remove duplicate rows from the merged result.
 1419: 
 1420:     # All unique tier values
 1421:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1422:         distinct => 1,
 1423:         columns  => ['tier'],
 1424:     );
 1425: 
 1426:     # All unique region + tier combinations
 1427:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1428:         distinct => 1,
 1429:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1430:     );
 1431: 
 1432:     # Remove fully-identical merged rows (all columns participate)
 1433:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1434: 
 1435: The optional C<columns> arrayref projects each row to the named columns I<before>
 1436: deduplication.  Omitting C<columns> (or passing an empty arrayref) deduplicates
 1437: on the full merged row: two rows must be identical in every column to be
 1438: considered duplicates.
 1439: 
 1440: B<Array backend>: deduplication is done in Perl using a seen-hash keyed on the
 1441: selected column values.  First occurrence wins.
 1442: 
 1443: B<SQLite backend>: C<SELECT DISTINCT col1, col2, ...> is used; the SQL engine
 1444: handles deduplication.
 1445: 
 1446: Both backends produce identical results.
 1447: 
 1448: B<Interaction with sort_by / limit / offset>: C<distinct> is applied first
 1449: (before pagination and ordering), so C<limit> and C<offset> operate on the
 1450: already-deduplicated set.
 1451: 
 1452: =head2 collision_prefix - preserve colliding columns from secondary databases
 1453: 
 1454: By default, when a column name appears in more than one database the I<last>
 1455: database wins: its value silently overwrites earlier ones in merged rows.
 1456: This loses data and makes the origin invisible.
 1457: 
 1458: C<collision_prefix> changes this for secondary databases you designate.
 1459: When a secondary database at index N has a column that already exists in the
 1460: merged view, and C<collision_prefix-E<gt>{N}> is set, the colliding column is
 1461: published as C<"$prefix.$col"> instead of overwriting.  Both values are then
 1462: visible: the original column keeps its name (from the earlier database), and
 1463: the collision gets the prefixed name.
 1464: 
 1465: C<collision_prefix> is a hashref.  Each B<key> is the B<zero-based index> of
 1466: a secondary database in the C<databases> array (same numbering as C<join_map>).
 1467: Each B<value> is the prefix string to prepend.  An index-0 entry is
 1468: meaningless and silently ignored.  Omitting C<collision_prefix> entirely
 1469: preserves the previous last-wins behaviour and changes nothing.
 1470: 
 1471: Non-colliding columns from a secondary database are always added as-is with
 1472: no prefix, whether or not C<collision_prefix> is configured.
 1473: 
 1474: B<Example -- sales table and products table, both with a "product" column>
 1475: 
 1476:     # $sales    columns: id, product, amount, date
 1477:     # $products columns: sku, product, price, category
 1478:     # join on 'product' (left key) matched against 'sku' (right key via join_map)
 1479: 
 1480:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1481:         databases        => [$sales, $products],
 1482:         join_column      => 'product',
 1483:         join_map         => { 1 => 'sku' },
 1484:         collision_prefix => { 1 => 'products' },
 1485:     );
 1486: 
 1487:     $join->columns;
 1488:     # => ['amount', 'category', 'date', 'id', 'price', 'product', 'products.product']
 1489:     #                                                               ^-- prefixed collision
 1490: 
 1491:     my $row = $join->fetchrow_hashref(product => 'widget');
 1492:     # $row->{product}            -- value from $sales
 1493:     # $row->{'products.product'} -- value from $products (different row, same column name)
 1494:     # $row->{price}              -- from $products, no collision, kept as-is
 1495: 
 1496: B<Querying on a prefixed column>
 1497: 
 1498: Use the full published name as the criterion key:
 1499: 
 1500:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('products.product' => 'widget');
 1501:     # Internally routes as: product => 'widget' to $products
 1502: 
 1503: B<Interaction with remove_column>
 1504: 
 1505: C<remove_column> operates on published names.  To suppress a prefixed
 1506: collision column entirely, pass the prefixed name:
 1507: 
 1508:     $join->remove_column('products.product');
 1509: 
 1510: =head2 backend - SQLite join backend for large datasets
 1511: 
 1512: C<Database::Join> can merge component databases in two different ways,
 1513: controlled by the C<backend> constructor parameter.
 1514: 
 1515: =over 4
 1516: 
 1517: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'array'> -- in-memory merge (original behaviour)
 1518: 
 1519: All matching rows are fetched from every component database into Perl hashes
 1520: and merged there.  Simple and fast for small and medium datasets.  Peak RAM
 1521: is roughly three times the combined source data size (one copy per database
 1522: plus one merged copy).
 1523: 
 1524: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'> -- SQL JOIN via a cached temporary file
 1525: 
 1526: C<Database::Join> creates a temporary SQLite database file, spills source
 1527: rows into it (one table per component database), then executes a single SQL
 1528: C<JOIN> statement per query call.  Peak RAM drops to roughly one times the
 1529: source data size.
 1530: 
 1531: The temporary file is created once and reused across multiple query calls on
 1532: the same object (the cache).  Only query-time criteria vary per call; they
 1533: are applied as SQL C<WHERE> clauses against the cached data.  The cache is
 1534: automatically rebuilt when any source database's C<updated()> timestamp
 1535: changes.  The file is deleted when the object is destroyed (goes out of
 1536: scope).  See I<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime> below for
 1537: details.
 1538: 
 1539: Requires C<DBD::SQLite E<gt>= 1.70> (C<FULL OUTER JOIN> support was added in
 1540: SQLite 3.39.0; DBD::SQLite 1.70 ships SQLite 3.39.2).
 1541: 
 1542: =item C<backend =E<gt> 'auto'> (default)
 1543: 
 1544: C<Database::Join> counts the total rows from all component databases cheaply
 1545: -- without fetching them -- and then decides:
 1546: 
 1547: =over 4
 1548: 
 1549: =item *
 1550: 
 1551: If the combined count is less than or equal to C<max_array_rows> (default
 1552: 10,000), use the array path.
 1553: 
 1554: =item *
 1555: 
 1556: If the combined count exceeds C<max_array_rows>, use the SQLite path.
 1557: 
 1558: =back
 1559: 
 1560: For counting to work without fetching, each component database must either
 1561: implement the C<dbi_source()> interface (for SQLite-backed sources, where a
 1562: C<COUNT(*)> SQL query is issued directly), or directly define a C<count()>
 1563: method in its own package -- not just inherit one from a parent class.  If
 1564: neither is available for a particular database, C<Database::Join> plays it
 1565: safe and uses the array path for the whole query without fetching any rows.
 1566: 
 1567: =back
 1568: 
 1569: B<Choosing max_array_rows>
 1570: 
 1571: The default of 10,000 is a reasonable starting point.  Adjust it to match
 1572: your hardware and typical row width.  For wide rows (many columns or long
 1573: strings) you may want a lower threshold; for narrow rows you can raise it.
 1574: 
 1575: B<Temporary file: name, location, and lifetime>
 1576: 
 1577: When the SQLite path is active, a single temporary SQLite database file acts
 1578: as the join cache for the life of the C<Database::Join> object.
 1579: 
 1580: B<Name>: the filename is randomly generated by C<File::Temp>, for example:
 1581: 
 1582:     /tmp/Cj8xK7mP2Q.db
 1583: 
 1584: The random portion (ten characters) is chosen automatically to avoid
 1585: collisions.  Only the directory is under your control; you cannot specify
 1586: the filename itself.
 1587: 
 1588: B<Location>: controlled by the C<tmpdir> constructor parameter.
 1589: If C<tmpdir> is not specified, C<File::Spec-E<gt>tmpdir()> is used (usually
 1590: C</tmp> on Unix, or the value of the C<TEMP> or C<TMP> environment variable
 1591: on Windows).
 1592: 
 1593: B<Lifetime: one file per object, deleted when the object is destroyed>: the
 1594: file is created on the first query call that uses the SQLite path and kept
 1595: alive until the C<Database::Join> object is destroyed (i.e. when it goes out
 1596: of scope or is explicitly C<undef>-d).  At most one file exists per object at
 1597: any given moment.  Calling C<selectall_arrayref()> ten times on the same
 1598: object creates and uses I<one> file, not ten.
 1599: 
 1600: B<Cache invalidation>: the cache is automatically rebuilt (the old file is
 1601: replaced with a new one) when any source database's C<updated()> return value
 1602: changes, or when C<add_database()> is called.  Base-filter criteria
 1603: (C<filters> constructor parameter) are applied once at build time for spilled
 1604: sources; query-time criteria are applied per-call as SQL C<WHERE> clauses.
 1605: 
 1606: To use a different directory -- for example a RAM-backed filesystem or a
 1607: faster local disk:
 1608: 
 1609:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1610:         databases      => [ $db1, $db2 ],
 1611:         join_column    => 'entry',
 1612:         backend        => 'sqlite',
 1613:         tmpdir         => '/dev/shm',     # Linux RAM disk
 1614:     );
 1615: 
 1616: B<Parallel secondary fetches (C<parallel> constructor parameter)>
 1617: 
 1618: By default, component databases are queried sequentially - the primary first,
 1619: then each secondary in order.  When the component databases are network- or
 1620: disk-backed and have non-trivial per-query latency, the sequential fetch means
 1621: total latency is the I<sum> of all per-DA latencies.
 1622: 
 1623: Setting C<< parallel => 1 >> in the constructor enables concurrent fetching
 1624: of secondary databases using Perl C<threads>.  With C<parallel => 1> and
 1625: two or more secondary databases (C<n E<gt> 2> total), secondary fetches run in
 1626: parallel after the primary fetch completes; total latency drops to
 1627: I<max(secondary latencies)> instead of I<sum(secondary latencies)>.
 1628: 
 1629:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 1630:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty, $scores ],   # 3 DAs - 2 secondaries
 1631:         join_column => 'entry',
 1632:         parallel    => 1,    # loyalty and scores fetched concurrently
 1633:     );
 1634: 
 1635: Requirements and caveats:
 1636: 
 1637: =over 4
 1638: 
 1639: =item *
 1640: 
 1641: The C<threads> module must be available.  Most distributions ship it, but it
 1642: requires a Perl binary compiled with C<-Dusethreads>.  When threads are
 1643: unavailable, a C<carp> warning is emitted and fetching falls back to
 1644: sequential; the result is identical, only slower.
 1645: 
 1646: =item *
 1647: 
 1648: Parallel fetching is only active when the join has three or more total
 1649: databases (C<n E<gt> 2>).  With two databases (one secondary), the thread
 1650: creation overhead exceeds the benefit of concurrency; sequential is used
 1651: regardless of C<parallel>.
 1652: 
 1653: =item *
 1654: 
 1655: Component databases must be safe to call from Perl threads.  In-memory
 1656: databases (CSV, JSON, TSV after slurp) are safe.  DBI-backed databases
 1657: whose handles were created in the same thread may not be safe - consult your
 1658: DBD driver's thread documentation.  The array backend is recommended for
 1659: DBI-backed sources; the SQLite backend performs its join in a single SQL
 1660: statement and does not use parallel fetching.
 1661: 
 1662: =item *
 1663: 
 1664: C<parallel => 1> applies to the array backend only.  The SQLite backend
 1665: performs a single SQL JOIN after spilling source data, so per-DA parallelism
 1666: is irrelevant.
 1667: 
 1668: =back
 1669: 
 1670: B<Zero-copy ATTACH (C<dbi_source()> interface)>
 1671: 
 1672: Normally, when the SQLite path is active, rows from each component database
 1673: are fetched one by one and inserted into the temporary SQLite file.  This is
 1674: efficient but does involve INSERT overhead.
 1675: 
 1676: If a component database is itself SQLite-backed and implements a
 1677: C<dbi_source()> method, C<Database::Join> can skip the row-by-row copy
 1678: entirely and instead use C<ATTACH DATABASE> to link the source file directly
 1679: to the temporary join connection.  This is the zero-copy path and is
 1680: significantly faster for large SQLite sources.
 1681: 
 1682: The C<dbi_source()> method must return a hashref with two keys:
 1683: 
 1684: =over 4
 1685: 
 1686: =item C<dbh>
 1687: 
 1688: A connected C<DBD::SQLite> database handle (C<DBI> connection object).
 1689: 
 1690: =item C<table>
 1691: 
 1692: The name of the table in that database that holds the source rows.
 1693: 
 1694: =back
 1695: 
 1696: Example implementation:
 1697: 
 1698:     package My::SQLiteDatabase;
 1699:     use parent 'Database::Abstraction';
 1700: 
 1701:     sub dbi_source {
 1702:         my ($self) = @_;
 1703:         return {
 1704:             dbh   => $self->{_dbh},      # connected DBD::SQLite handle
 1705:             table => $self->{_table},    # table name in that database
 1706:         };
 1707:     }
 1708: 
 1709:     1;
 1710: 
 1711: The zero-copy ATTACH path is always used when a component database implements
 1712: C<dbi_source()>.  Query-time criteria are applied as parameterised SQL
 1713: C<WHERE> clauses against the ATTACHed source table, so no row-level copy is
 1714: needed even when the current call includes column filters.  (Prior to 0.005.0
 1715: any query-time criteria forced a spill; that restriction was removed in
 1716: 0.005.0.)
 1717: 
 1718: B<Result identity>
 1719: 
 1720: Both the array path and the SQLite path produce identical results for any
 1721: given query.  You can switch between them freely without changing callers.
 1722: The C<collision_prefix> column renaming, C<join_map> key translation, and all
 1723: three join types (left, inner, outer) work identically on both paths.
 1724: 
 1725: =head2 selectall_arrayref
 1726: 
 1727: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1728: 
 1729:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1730:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier  => 'gold');
 1731:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1732:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref('C001');  # positional: entry => 'C001'
 1733:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(sort_by => 'name');
 1734:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => ['score', 'DESC']);
 1735:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10);
 1736:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(limit => 10, offset => 20);
 1737:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold', sort_by => 'name', limit => 5);
 1738:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1739:     my $rows = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);  # distinct on all columns
 1740: 
 1741: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1742: 
 1743: Returns an arrayref of hashrefs representing the merged view of all component
 1744: databases, optionally filtered by the given criteria.
 1745: 
 1746: Criteria for columns that live in different databases are routed
 1747: automatically: each database is queried with only the criteria that apply to
 1748: its own columns.  The results are combined in memory using C<join_column>.
 1749: Under a C<left> or C<inner> join, a secondary database is also limited to the
 1750: primary's keys (see "Key pushdown" in L</DESCRIPTION>).
 1751: 
 1752: Accepts the same criteria syntax as C<Database::Abstraction::selectall_arrayref>.
 1753: A single plain scalar argument is interpreted as the C<join_column> value
 1754: (equivalent to C<< entry => 'C001' >> when C<join_column> is C<'entry'>).
 1755: 
 1756: Pass C<< distinct => 1 >> (with optional C<< columns => \@cols >>) to
 1757: deduplicate results; see the C<distinct> feature section for details.
 1758: 
 1759: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1760: 
 1761: =head4 Input
 1762: 
 1763:     Calling conventions (in order of precedence):
 1764:       1. No arguments             -- returns all rows
 1765:       2. One plain scalar         -- shorthand for join_column => $scalar
 1766:       3. Key-value pairs or
 1767:          a criteria hashref       -- routed per-database
 1768: 
 1769:     Values may be:
 1770:       Plain scalar                -- exact match
 1771:       Hashref of operators        -- e.g. { '>' => 80 }
 1772: 
 1773:     Optional parameters (mixed in with any of the above):
 1774:       sort_by => 'colname'            -- sort ascending by that column
 1775:       sort_by => ['colname', 'DESC']  -- sort descending
 1776:       sort_by => ['colname', 'ASC']   -- sort ascending (explicit)
 1777:       limit    => N                    -- return at most N rows (positive integer)
 1778:       offset   => M                   -- skip the first M rows (non-negative integer)
 1779: 
 1780:     The column named in sort_by must be present in the merged view (i.e. it
 1781:     must appear in columns()).  An unknown column or an invalid direction emits
 1782:     a carp warning and falls back to the default join_column ascending sort.
 1783: 
 1784:     limit and offset are applied after ordering.  offset without limit skips
 1785:     rows but returns all remaining rows.  limit without offset starts from
 1786:     the first qualifying row.  An invalid limit or offset emits a carp warning
 1787:     and the parameter is ignored (treated as absent).
 1788: 
 1789:     DOMAIN -- sort_by:
 1790:       EP absent:          result sorted by join_column ASC (default).
 1791:       EP string:          any column name in columns(); sorts ASC by that column.
 1792:       EP ['col','ASC']:   explicit ascending; equivalent to the string form.
 1793:       EP ['col','DESC']:  descending sort by the named column.
 1794:       EP ['col']:         single-element arrayref; direction defaults to ASC.
 1795:       EP []:              empty arrayref; column is undef -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1796:       EP invalid column:  column not in columns() -> carp + join_col ASC fallback.
 1797:       EP invalid dir:     direction not 'ASC' or 'DESC' -> carp + ASC used.
 1798:       Sort is lexicographic (cmp); use backend=>'sqlite' for numeric ORDER BY.
 1799: 
 1800:     DOMAIN -- limit:
 1801:       EP absent:          no truncation; all qualifying rows are returned.
 1802:       EP 0:               not a positive integer -> carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1803:       BVA min valid = 1:  exactly 1 row returned.
 1804:       BVA at count:       limit == total rows -> all rows returned (no truncation).
 1805:       BVA above count:    limit > total rows -> all rows returned.
 1806:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1807:                           => carp + ignored (all rows returned).
 1808:       Valid domain:       integers in [1, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a with value >= 1.
 1809: 
 1810:     DOMAIN -- offset:
 1811:       EP absent:          no rows skipped; result starts from row 0.
 1812:       BVA min valid = 0:  no rows skipped (zero is a valid non-negative integer).
 1813:       BVA offset=1:       first row skipped; result starts from row 1.
 1814:       BVA offset=N-1:     N-1 rows skipped; only the last row returned.
 1815:       BVA offset=N:       all N rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1816:       BVA offset>N:       all rows skipped; empty result returned.
 1817:       EP invalid:         negative integer, float string, or non-numeric string
 1818:                           => carp + ignored (no rows skipped).
 1819:       Valid domain:       integers in [0, INF); matched by /^\d+\z/a.
 1820: 
 1821:     distinct => 1          -- deduplicate rows after the merge (see columns below)
 1822:     columns  => \@cols     -- arrayref of column names to project and deduplicate on;
 1823:                               used together with distinct => 1
 1824: 
 1825:     DOMAIN -- distinct:
 1826:       EP absent or 0:     no deduplication; all merged rows are returned.
 1827:       EP 1:               duplicates are removed (see below); first occurrence wins.
 1828:       EP truthy (not 1):  treated as truthy; deduplication is applied.
 1829:       Effect:             on the array backend, a Perl seen-hash keyed on the
 1830:                           selected column values is used; first occurrence wins.
 1831:                           On the SQLite backend, SELECT DISTINCT is used.
 1832: 
 1833:     DOMAIN -- columns:
 1834:       EP absent/undef:    when distinct is set, deduplication is on all columns
 1835:                           and full rows are returned.
 1836:       EP []:              empty arrayref treated the same as absent.
 1837:       EP valid:           arrayref of column names present in the merged view;
 1838:                           result rows are projected to those columns only, and
 1839:                           deduplication is on the projected values.
 1840:       EP with distinct=0: columns is ignored when distinct is absent or false.
 1841:       Note:               unknown column names in the list are silently ignored
 1842:                           at projection time; the row may lack those keys.
 1843: 
 1844: =head4 Output
 1845: 
 1846:     Arrayref of hashrefs; one hashref per qualifying merged row.
 1847:     Sorted ascending by join_column by default; caller-controlled via sort_by.
 1848:     At most C<limit> rows when limit is given; the first C<offset> rows are
 1849:     skipped when offset is given.
 1850:     Returns a reference to an empty array when no rows match.
 1851: 
 1852: =head3 EXAMPLE
 1853: 
 1854:     # All rows from both databases
 1855:     my $all = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 1856: 
 1857:     # Only rows where the 'tier' column (from the loyalty database)
 1858:     # equals 'gold' -- the criterion is routed to the right database
 1859:     my $vip = $join->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 1860: 
 1861:     # Operator hashref: score > 80
 1862:     my $high = $join->selectall_arrayref(score => { '>' => 80 });
 1863: 
 1864:     # Access each merged row
 1865:     for my $row (@{$vip}) {
 1866:         printf "%-10s tier=%-8s score=%d\n",
 1867:             $row->{entry}, $row->{tier}, $row->{score} // 0;
 1868:     }
 1869: 
 1870:     # Distinct tier values across all rows (project to one column, deduplicate)
 1871:     my $tiers = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1872:     # Returns e.g. [{ tier => 'gold' }, { tier => 'silver' }, { tier => 'bronze' }]
 1873:     # regardless of how many rows exist for each tier
 1874: 
 1875:     # Distinct region+tier combinations
 1876:     my $combos = $join->selectall_arrayref(
 1877:         distinct => 1,
 1878:         columns  => ['region', 'tier'],
 1879:     );
 1880: 
 1881:     # Distinct on all columns (remove fully-duplicate merged rows)
 1882:     my $deduped = $join->selectall_arrayref(distinct => 1);
 1883: 
 1884: =head3 MESSAGES
 1885: 
 1886:     warn_unknown_column (carp)
 1887:         -- A criterion key names a column not present in any component database;
 1888:            the criterion is silently dropped and all rows are returned.
 1889:     error_join_criterion (croak)
 1890:         -- A criterion hashref contains a join => key (DA SQL-JOIN syntax).
 1891:            Use a separate DA object for the joined table instead.
 1892:     sort_by column unknown (carp)
 1893:         -- The column given in sort_by is not in the merged view; the result
 1894:            is returned in the default join_column ascending order instead.
 1895:     sort_by direction invalid (carp)
 1896:         -- The direction given in sort_by is not 'ASC' or 'DESC'; ASC is used.
 1897:     limit invalid (carp)
 1898:         -- The value given for limit is not a positive integer; it is ignored.
 1899:     offset invalid (carp)
 1900:         -- The value given for offset is not a non-negative integer; it is ignored.
 1901:     operator-unsupported (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1902:         -- An operator hashref key is not in the supported set (>, <, >=, <=,
 1903:            !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE, IS NULL, IS NOT NULL, IN, NOT IN); the
 1904:            individual operator term is dropped from the WHERE clause (other
 1905:            operators in the same hashref still apply).
 1906:     IN/NOT IN wrong value type (carp, SQLite/auto path only)
 1907:         -- An IN or NOT IN criterion was given a non-arrayref value; the operator
 1908:            term is skipped.
 1909:     error_sqlite_connect (croak, SQLite/auto path only)
 1910:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created; check tmpdir
 1911:            permissions and available disk space.
 1912: 
 1913: =cut
 1914: 
 1915: sub selectall_arrayref {
 1916: 	my ($self, @args) = @_;
 1917: 	my $params   = $self->_parse_query_args(undef, @args);
 1918: 	my $sort_by  = delete $params->{sort_by};
 1919: 	my $limit    = delete $params->{limit};
 1920: 	my $offset   = delete $params->{offset};
 1921: 	my $distinct = delete $params->{distinct};
 1922: 	my $columns  = delete $params->{columns};
 1923: 	return $self->_joined_query($params,

  Mutants (Total: 2, Killed: 2, Survived: 0)
 1924: 		sort_by  => $sort_by, limit => $limit, offset => $offset,
 1925: 		distinct => $distinct, columns => $columns);
 1926: }
 1927: 
 1928: =head2 selectall_array
 1929: 
 1930: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 1931: 
 1932:     my @rows = $join->selectall_array(tier => 'gold');
 1933: 
 1934:     # Scalar context: only the first matching row
 1935:     my $first = $join->selectall_array(entry => 'C001');
 1936: 
 1937:     # Distinct column projection (list context)
 1938:     my @tiers = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1, columns => ['tier']);
 1939: 
 1940:     # Distinct on all columns
 1941:     my @deduped = $join->selectall_array(distinct => 1);
 1942: 
 1943: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 1944: 
 1945: In list context returns a list of merged hashrefs -- the same rows that
 1946: C<selectall_arrayref> would return, just as a flat list rather than an
 1947: arrayref.
 1948: 
 1949: In scalar context returns only the first matching hashref (or C<undef> if
 1950: nothing matches).
 1951: 
 1952: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 1953:

 2125: =head2 aggregate
 2126: 
 2127: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2128: 
 2129:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2130:         by       => ['tier'],
 2131:         measures => [
 2132:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum', as => 'total' },
 2133:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'   },
 2134:         ],
 2135:         having   => { total => { '>' => 100 } },
 2136:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2137:         limit    => 10,
 2138:     );
 2139:     # => [ { tier => 'gold', total => 183, n => 2 }, ... ]
 2140: 
 2141: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2142: 
 2143: Groups the merged view by one or more published column names and computes
 2144: aggregate measures over each group.
 2145: 
 2146: On the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'sqlite'>) and the C<auto> backend,
 2147: the aggregation is pushed into the existing SQLite cache as a C<GROUP BY>
 2148: query - rows never pass through Perl.  The C<auto> backend always takes this
 2149: path for aggregation, skipping the row-count threshold probe (aggregation
 2150: makes the row count irrelevant).
 2151: 
 2152: On the array backend (C<backend =E<gt> 'array'>), merged rows are fetched in
 2153: full and folded in Perl.  This materialises the complete matching result set.
 2154: 
 2155: The two paths return the same rows for the same input, except for two
 2156: documented differences: C<min>/C<max> on a column declared C<TEXT> but
 2157: storing numbers (SQL compares lexically; Perl detects numerics), and
 2158: C<sum>/C<avg> with non-numeric values (SQL coerces to 0.0; Perl skips and
 2159: warns via C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>).
 2160: 
 2161: C<Database::Join> has no C<no_fixate> and no CHI cache (unlike
 2162: C<Database::Abstraction>); aggregation results are not cached between calls.
 2163: 
 2164: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2165: 
 2166: =head4 Input
 2167: 
 2168: Reserved keys extracted before the rest is treated as WHERE criteria:
 2169: 
 2170:     by       => arrayref of published column names (default: [] = grand total)
 2171:     measures => arrayref of measure hashrefs (required, non-empty)
 2172:     having   => hashref of post-aggregation criteria
 2173:     sort_by  => 'col' or ['col', 'DESC']
 2174:     limit    => positive integer
 2175:     offset   => non-negative integer
 2176: 
 2177: Every other key is pre-aggregation criteria routed exactly as
 2178: C<selectall_arrayref> routes criteria.
 2179: 
 2180: Measure hashref keys:
 2181: 
 2182:     fn  => function name (sum|avg|min|max|count|count_distinct), case-insensitive
 2183:     col => published column name (required for all functions except bare count)
 2184:     as  => result key (required; must match C</^\w+\z/>; unique; not a by name)
 2185: 
 2186: C<having> uses the same operator vocabulary as C<selectall_arrayref>:
 2187: C<%SAFE_SQL_OPS> (E<gt>, E<lt>, E<gt>=, E<lt>=, !=, =, LIKE, NOT LIKE),
 2188: C<%SAFE_LIST_OPS> (IN, NOT IN), C<%SAFE_NOARG_OPS> (IS NULL, IS NOT NULL),
 2189: a bare scalar (= ?), and C<-or>/C<-and> groupings.  Write C<-between> as
 2190: two operators and C<LIKE> explicitly.
 2191: 
 2192: =head4 Output
 2193: 
 2194: Always an arrayref of hashrefs (never C<undef>).  C<[]> when no group
 2195: qualifies and C<by> is non-empty.  When C<by> is omitted or C<[]> and no
 2196: rows match, returns one all-normalised row (grand total of zero).
 2197: 
 2198: Key order per row: C<by> columns in declaration order, then measure aliases
 2199: in declaration order.
 2200: 
 2201: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2202: 
 2203:     # Total score and row count per tier, highest total first
 2204:     my $rows = $join->aggregate(
 2205:         by       => ['tier'],
 2206:         measures => [
 2207:             { col => 'score', fn => 'sum',   as => 'total' },
 2208:             {                 fn => 'count', as => 'n'     },
 2209:         ],
 2210:         sort_by  => ['total', 'DESC'],
 2211:     );
 2212:     for my $r (@{$rows}) {
 2213:         printf "%s: total=%s n=%s\n", $r->{tier}, $r->{total}, $r->{n};
 2214:     }
 2215: 
 2216: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2217: 
 2218:     1. get_params(undef, @args) -- hashref or kv-pairs only (no positional key)
 2219:     2. croak error_join_criterion if 'join =>' present
 2220:     3. _validate_aggregate_spec extracts by/measures/having/sort_by/limit/offset
 2221:     4. array backend:
 2222:          rows   = _joined_query_array(remaining criteria, no sort/limit/offset)
 2223:          groups = _fold_aggregate(rows, spec)
 2224:          groups = [grep { _match_criteria($_, having) } @groups] if having
 2225:          _normalise_aggregate(groups, spec.measures)
 2226:          _sort_and_page(groups, spec)
 2227:        sqlite/auto backend:
 2228:          _sqlite_aggregate(remaining criteria, spec)
 2229:     5. return groups
 2230: 
 2231: =head3 MESSAGES
 2232: 
 2233: C<error_aggregate_measures_required>, C<error_aggregate_measure_ref>,
 2234: C<error_aggregate_fn_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_col_required>,
 2235: C<error_aggregate_col_unknown>, C<error_aggregate_as_required>,
 2236: C<error_aggregate_as_invalid>, C<error_aggregate_as_duplicate>,
 2237: C<error_aggregate_as_collision>, C<error_aggregate_by_invalid>,
 2238: C<error_aggregate_having_key>, C<error_aggregate_having_operator>,
 2239: C<warn_aggregate_sort_key>, C<warn_aggregate_non_numeric>.
 2240: 
 2241: =cut
 2242:

 2275: =head2 each_row
 2276: 
 2277: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2278: 
 2279:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { my ($row) = @_; ... });
 2280:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, tier => 'gold');
 2281:     my $count = $join->each_row(sub { ... }, sort_by => 'name', limit => 100);
 2282: 
 2283: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2284: 
 2285: Calls C<\&callback> once for every row in the merged view that matches the
 2286: given criteria, then returns the total count of rows visited.
 2287: 
 2288: Accepts the same criteria, C<sort_by>, C<limit>, and C<offset> parameters as
 2289: C<selectall_arrayref>.
 2290: 
 2291: B<Note on memory usage>: C<Database::Join> always materialises the complete
 2292: merged result set before invoking the callback, because merging rows from
 2293: multiple independent sources requires that every source be queried and
 2294: cross-referenced first.  True constant-memory streaming (as C<Database::Abstraction>
 2295: provides on single-source SQL queries) is not possible at the join layer.
 2296: For genuinely large result sets use the SQLite backend (C<backend =E<gt>
 2297: 'sqlite'>), which keeps peak RAM to approximately one times the source data
 2298: size rather than three.
 2299: 
 2300: Any exception thrown inside the callback propagates to the caller after the
 2301: current row; subsequent rows are not visited.
 2302: 
 2303: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2304: 
 2305: =head4 Input
 2306: 
 2307:     \&callback   Positional coderef (required).
 2308:                  Called as $callback->($row_hashref) for each merged row.
 2309: 
 2310:     Additional arguments follow the same calling conventions as
 2311:     selectall_arrayref: no args, a single scalar join-column value,
 2312:     or key-value criteria pairs (including sort_by, limit, offset).
 2313: 
 2314: =head4 Output
 2315: 
 2316:     Non-negative integer: the count of rows for which the callback was invoked.
 2317: 
 2318: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2319: 
 2320:     # Print every gold-tier customer's name, sorted by name
 2321:     my $count = $join->each_row(
 2322:         sub { my ($row) = @_; print "$row->{name}\n" },
 2323:         tier    => 'gold',
 2324:         sort_by => 'name',
 2325:     );
 2326:     print "$count gold-tier customers\n";
 2327: 
 2328:     # Accumulate without holding the full result
 2329:     my $total_score = 0;
 2330:     $join->each_row(sub { $total_score += $_[0]->{score} // 0 });
 2331: 
 2332: =head3 MESSAGES
 2333: 
 2334:     error_invalid_callback (croak)
 2335:         -- First argument is not a code reference.
 2336: 
 2337: =cut
 2338:

 2355: =head2 dbi_source
 2356: 
 2357: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2358: 
 2359:     # Use a Database::Join object as a zero-copy SQLite source inside a
 2360:     # parent Database::Join, giving the parent ATTACHed-speed access to
 2361:     # the child's joined data without iterating through Perl.
 2362:     my $child  = Database::Join->new(databases => [$da, $db], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2363:     my $parent = Database::Join->new(databases => [$child, $dc], join_column => 'id', backend => 'sqlite');
 2364: 
 2365: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2366: 
 2367: Returns a hashref C<< { dbh => $sqlite_dbh, table => '_dj_result' } >> that
 2368: allows a parent C<Database::Join> (or any other caller that understands the
 2369: C<dbi_source()> interface) to ATTACH the child's temporary SQLite database
 2370: file and query the materialised join result directly via SQL, without routing
 2371: rows through Perl.
 2372: 
 2373: The first call builds the SQLite cache (if not already current) and
 2374: materialises the full join result - with C<filters> applied but no query-time
 2375: criteria - into a real table named C<_dj_result> inside the cache file.
 2376: Subsequent calls within the same cache cycle reuse the existing table.
 2377: 
 2378: Returns C<undef> when the backend is C<'array'> (no SQLite file exists).
 2379: 
 2380: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2381: 
 2382: =head4 Input
 2383: 
 2384:     None.
 2385: 
 2386: =head4 Output
 2387: 
 2388:     On the SQLite/auto backend:
 2389:       Hashref with keys:
 2390:         dbh   => DBI handle to the child's temporary SQLite database file.
 2391:         table => '_dj_result'  (the materialized join table inside that file).
 2392:     On the array backend:
 2393:       undef
 2394: 
 2395: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2396: 
 2397:     # The parent automatically ATTACHes the child's SQLite file and queries
 2398:     # _dj_result for zero-copy composable nested joins.
 2399:     my $inner = Database::Join->new(
 2400:         databases   => [$customers, $loyalty],
 2401:         join_column => 'entry',
 2402:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2403:     );
 2404:     my $outer = Database::Join->new(
 2405:         databases   => [$inner, $scores],
 2406:         join_column => 'entry',
 2407:         backend     => 'sqlite',
 2408:     );
 2409:     my $rows = $outer->selectall_arrayref(tier => 'gold');
 2410: 
 2411: =head3 MESSAGES
 2412: 
 2413:     error_sqlite_connect (croak)
 2414:         -- The temporary SQLite join file could not be created.
 2415: 
 2416: =cut
 2417:

 2445: =head2 columns
 2446: 
 2447: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2448: 
 2449:     my $cols = $join->columns();
 2450:

 2452: 
 2453: Returns an arrayref of all column names visible in the merged view,
 2454: deduplicated and sorted alphabetically.
 2455: 
 2456: The C<join_column> appears exactly once, even if it exists under different
 2457: local names in some databases (see C<join_map>).  Columns that have been
 2458: hidden with C<remove_column> or C<remove_columns> do not appear.
 2459:

 2478: =head3 MESSAGES
 2479: 
 2480: C<columns()> does not itself emit any warnings or errors.  Any exception thrown
 2481: by a component database's C<columns()> method propagates uncaught.

 2513: =head2 schema
 2514: 
 2515: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2516: 
 2517:     my $schema = $join->schema();
 2518: 
 2519: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2520: 
 2521: Returns a merged schema hashref for all visible columns across all component
 2522: databases.  Each key is a column name; each value is the schema metadata
 2523: hashref returned by C<Database::Abstraction::schema()> for that column
 2524: (typically C<{ type, nullable, default, pk }>).
 2525: 
 2526: When the same column name appears in more than one database the I<last>
 2527: database's metadata is used.  Columns hidden with C<remove_column> are not
 2528: included.
 2529: 
 2530: The result is memoised.
 2531: 
 2532: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2533: 
 2534: =head4 Input
 2535: 
 2536:     None.
 2537: 
 2538: =head4 Output
 2539: 
 2540:     Hashref: column_name => { type => ..., nullable => ..., default => ..., pk => ... }.
 2541: 
 2542: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2543: 
 2544:     my $schema = $join->schema();
 2545:     for my $col (sort keys %{$schema}) {
 2546:         my $info = $schema->{$col};
 2547:         printf "%-15s type=%-10s nullable=%s\n",
 2548:             $col, $info->{type}, $info->{nullable} ? 'yes' : 'no';
 2549:     }
 2550: 
 2551: =head3 MESSAGES
 2552: 
 2553: C<schema()> does not itself emit any warnings or errors.  Any exception thrown
 2554: by a component database's C<schema()> method propagates uncaught.
 2555: 
 2556: =cut

 2590: =head2 updated
 2591: 
 2592: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2593: 
 2594:     my $ts = $join->updated();
 2595: 
 2596: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2597: 
 2598: Returns the Unix timestamp of the most recent modification across all
 2599: component databases.  This is the maximum of all individual C<updated()>
 2600: return values.
 2601: 
 2602: Use this to implement simple cache-invalidation logic: if C<updated()>
 2603: has advanced since your last snapshot, re-query.
 2604: 
 2605: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2606: 
 2607: =head4 Input
 2608: 
 2609:     None.
 2610: 
 2611: =head4 Output
 2612: 
 2613:     Unix timestamp (positive integer).
 2614: 
 2615: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2616: 
 2617:     my $last_modified = $join->updated();
 2618:     if ($last_modified > $my_cache_timestamp) {
 2619:         $my_cache = $join->selectall_arrayref();
 2620:         $my_cache_timestamp = $last_modified;
 2621:     }
 2622: 
 2623: =head3 MESSAGES
 2624: 
 2625: C<updated()> does not emit any warnings or errors.  Component databases that do
 2626: not implement C<updated()>, or whose C<updated()> throws, are silently skipped;
 2627: only defined return values contribute to the maximum.  If no component database
 2628: implements C<updated()>, C<undef> is returned (same as C<List::Util::max> on an
 2629: empty list).
 2630: 
 2631: =cut

 2711: =head2 add_database
 2712: 
 2713: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2714: 
 2715:     # Positional: database object as first argument
 2716:     $join->add_database($db);
 2717: 
 2718:     # Named: equivalent to the above
 2719:     $join->add_database(database => $db);
 2720: 
 2721:     # With options (mixed positional + named)
 2722:     $join->add_database($db, remove_columns => ['internal_id']);
 2723:     $join->add_database($db, join_column    => 'local_key_name');
 2724:     $join->add_database($db, filter         => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2725: 
 2726:     # Chainable
 2727:     $join->add_database($db1)->add_database($db2, remove_columns => ['notes']);
 2728: 
 2729: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2730: 
 2731: Adds one more C<Database::Abstraction> subclass object to the logical view
 2732: and immediately updates the column-ownership index.
 2733: 
 2734: After the call, all query methods return rows that include columns from the
 2735: newly added database, and criteria on those new columns are routed to it
 2736: automatically.
 2737: 
 2738: When a column name in the new database already exists in an earlier database,
 2739: the new database becomes the authoritative source for that column
 2740: (last-database-wins, the same rule that applies at construction time).
 2741: 
 2742: The join-column must be present in the new database (or declared via
 2743: C<join_column>).  The logger is propagated to the new database if one is set.
 2744: 
 2745: C<add_database> is the runtime equivalent of listing the database in the
 2746: C<databases> array to C<new>.  The optional C<join_column> parameter is
 2747: equivalent to a C<join_map> entry; the optional C<filter> parameter is
 2748: equivalent to a C<filters> entry.
 2749: 
 2750: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2751: 
 2752: =head4 Input
 2753: 
 2754:     database       => { type => 'object',   required => 1 }
 2755:                       # A Database::Abstraction subclass instance.
 2756:                       #
 2757:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   blessed object that passes
 2758:                       #                       isa('Database::Abstraction').
 2759:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: non-reference, unblessed ref, wrong class,
 2760:                       #                       or non-reference non-key scalar (the guard at
 2761:                       #                       the top of add_database rejects it with
 2762:                       #                       error_invalid_db before validate_strict runs).
 2763: 
 2764:     join_column    => { type => 'string',   optional => 1 }
 2765:                       # The name of the join key in THIS new database,
 2766:                       # when it differs from the canonical join_column.
 2767:                       #
 2768:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string that exists as a column in the
 2769:                       #                       new database.
 2770:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: string absent from the new database's columns()
 2771:                       #                       => croak error_join_col_missing.
 2772: 
 2773:     filter         => { type => 'hashref',  optional => 1 }
 2774:                       # Permanent criteria for this database only.
 2775:                       # Same format as selectall_arrayref.
 2776:                       #
 2777:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   hashref of criteria (may be {} for no-op).
 2778:                       # DOMAIN -- EP absent:  no permanent filter applied; all rows visible.
 2779:                       # DOMAIN -- Key-set:    a non-empty filter makes this DB an inner-join
 2780:                       #                       partner regardless of the outer join_type.
 2781: 
 2782:     remove_columns => { type => 'arrayref', optional => 1 }
 2783:                       # Column names from this database to hide.
 2784:                       #
 2785:                       # DOMAIN -- EP valid:   arrayref of strings; non-existent columns silently
 2786:                       #                       ignored; empty [] is a safe no-op.
 2787:                       # DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column itself => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2788: 
 2789: =head4 Output
 2790: 
 2791:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 2792: 
 2793: =head3 EXAMPLE
 2794: 
 2795:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 2796:         databases   => [ $customers ],
 2797:         join_column => 'entry',
 2798:     );
 2799: 
 2800:     # Add loyalty data; hide internal columns from it
 2801:     $join->add_database($loyalty, remove_columns => ['audit_ts']);
 2802: 
 2803:     # Add score data; only include rows with score > 60
 2804:     $join->add_database($scores, filter => { score => { '>' => 60 } });
 2805: 
 2806:     # Add a database whose join key has a different local name
 2807:     $join->add_database($stnames, join_column => 'state_code');
 2808: 
 2809:     # All three options combined, and chained
 2810:     $join->add_database($db4,
 2811:         join_column    => 'ref_id',
 2812:         filter         => { active => 1 },
 2813:         remove_columns => ['legacy_col'],
 2814:     );
 2815: 
 2816: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 2817: 
 2818:     determine the new database's index (length of current _dbs array)
 2819:     extract the database object from positional or named argument
 2820:     croak if it is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2821:     register join_column alias in _join_map if different from canonical
 2822:     register filter in _filters if provided
 2823:     fetch column list from the new database
 2824:     croak if the join key is missing from the new database
 2825:     append the new database to _dbs and _db_cols
 2826:     update _col_db: for each new column, point it at the new index
 2827:         (last-database-wins; skip removed columns and the local join alias)
 2828:     invalidate _col_cache and _schema_cache
 2829:     propagate logger if set
 2830:     apply remove_columns if provided
 2831:     return $self
 2832: 
 2833: =head3 MESSAGES
 2834: 
 2835:     error_invalid_db          -- argument is not a Database::Abstraction subclass
 2836:     error_join_col_missing    -- join_column not found in the new database
 2837:     warn_schema_type_mismatch -- (carp) the new database has a shared column whose type
 2838:                                  differs from the type already in the view; use
 2839:                                  collision_prefix to preserve both values
 2840: 
 2841: =cut

 2949: =head2 remove_column
 2950: 
 2951: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 2952: 
 2953:     $join->remove_column('email');
 2954: 
 2955:     # Chainable
 2956:     $join->remove_column('internal_id')->remove_column('audit_ts');
 2957: 
 2958: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 2959: 
 2960: Permanently hides a column from the merged view.  After this call:
 2961: 
 2962: =over 4
 2963: 
 2964: =item *
 2965: 
 2966: The column does not appear in C<columns()> or C<schema()>.
 2967: 
 2968: =item *
 2969: 
 2970: Returned row hashrefs do not contain the column key.
 2971: 
 2972: =item *
 2973: 
 2974: Any query criterion that references the removed column is silently dropped
 2975: (with a C<carp> warning).
 2976: 
 2977: =back
 2978: 
 2979: The C<join_column> cannot be removed; attempting to do so will C<croak>.
 2980: Removing a column that does not exist in any database is silently ignored
 2981: (the call is idempotent and safe).  The C<columns()> and C<schema()>
 2982: memoisation caches are cleared automatically.
 2983: 
 2984: =head3 API SPECIFICATION
 2985: 
 2986: =head4 Input
 2987: 
 2988:     $col    Positional string: the column name to remove.
 2989: 
 2990:             DOMAIN -- EP valid:   any string; non-existent columns are silently
 2991:                                   ignored (idempotent call, returns $self).
 2992:             DOMAIN -- EP invalid: join_column value => croak error_remove_join_col.
 2993:             DOMAIN -- BVA:        undef and '' are explicit no-ops (returns $self).
 2994:                                   These are below the minimum meaningful string
 2995:                                   length and are handled without any warning.
 2996: 
 2997: =head4 Output
 2998: 
 2999:     Returns C<$self> to support method chaining.
 3000: 
 3001: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3002: 
 3003:     # Hide private fields immediately after construction
 3004:     my $join = Database::Join->new(
 3005:         databases   => [ $customers, $loyalty ],
 3006:         join_column => 'entry',

 3008:      ->remove_column('internal_notes');

 3010:     # Verify they are gone
 3011:     my $cols = $join->columns();

 3013: 
 3014: =head3 MESSAGES
 3015: 
 3016:     error_remove_join_col -- attempt to remove the join_column itself
 3017: 
 3018: =cut

 3041: =head2 query
 3042: 
 3043: Not supported.  C<Database::Join> does not implement the
 3044: C<Database::Abstraction::Query> chained builder because the builder's
 3045: C<.all()> / C<.first()> methods would be targeting a single component DA
 3046: rather than the merged view.  Calling this method will always C<croak>.
 3047: 
 3048: Use C<selectall_arrayref>, C<selectall_array>, C<fetchrow_hashref>,
 3049: C<count>, or C<each_row> against the C<Database::Join> object instead.
 3050: 
 3051: =cut

 3058: =head2 group_by
 3059: 
 3060: Not supported.  C<Database::Join> does not implement the
 3061: C<Database::Abstraction::Query> fluent chain entry point because the chain's
 3062: C<.all()> / C<.first()> methods would target a single component DA rather
 3063: than the merged view.  Calling this method will always C<croak>.
 3064: 
 3065: Use C<aggregate()> against the C<Database::Join> object instead.
 3066: 
 3067: =cut

 3087: 
 3088: =head2 AUTOLOAD - column shortcut
 3089: 
 3090: Calling an unknown method whose name matches a visible column name performs
 3091: a column lookup across the merged view.
 3092: 
 3093: =head3 SYNOPSIS
 3094: 
 3095:     # Scalar context: value from the first matching row
 3096:     my $name  = $join->name(entry => 'C001');
 3097: 
 3098:     # List context: values from every matching row
 3099:     my @tiers = $join->tier();
 3100: 
 3101:     # With a positional join-key argument (when join_column is 'entry')
 3102:     my $score = $join->score('C001');
 3103: 
 3104: =head3 DESCRIPTION
 3105: 
 3106: AUTOLOAD routes the call to the appropriate component database by looking up
 3107: the column name in the internal column-ownership index.
 3108: 
 3109: When either C<join_map> or C<filters> is active, AUTOLOAD performs a full
 3110: join query instead of delegating directly to the owning database.  This is
 3111: necessary because:
 3112: 
 3113: =over 4
 3114: 
 3115: =item *
 3116: 
 3117: With C<join_map>, the owning database's primary key may differ from the
 3118: canonical join key used in the call arguments.
 3119: 
 3120: =item *
 3121: 
 3122: With C<filters>, bypassing the join would return rows that the filter is
 3123: meant to exclude.
 3124: 
 3125: =back
 3126: 
 3127: In list context, every matching merged row contributes one value to the
 3128: returned list.  In scalar context, only the first row's value is returned.
 3129: 
 3130: Calling a method whose name begins with C<_> (a private method) via AUTOLOAD
 3131: will C<croak> with a clear error message rather than being silently ignored.
 3132: 
 3133: =head3 EXAMPLE
 3134: 
 3135:     # Lookup a single customer's name (scalar context)
 3136:     my $name = $join->name('C001');   # 'C001' maps to entry => 'C001'
 3137:     print "Name: $name\n";
 3138: 
 3139:     # Get every tier value in the view (list context)
 3140:     my @all_tiers = $join->tier();
 3141:     my %freq;
 3142:     $freq{$_}++ for @all_tiers;
 3143: 
 3144:     # join_map active: AUTOLOAD runs a full join so the criteria are
 3145:     # translated correctly between the canonical and local key names.
 3146:     my @leesburg_states = sort $join->state('Leesburg');
 3147:     # ['Florida', 'Virginia'] if Leesburg appears in two states
 3148: 
 3149: =head3 PSEUDOCODE
 3150: 
 3151:     extract column name from $AUTOLOAD
 3152:     return if DESTROY
 3153:     croak if column name starts with '_' (private method guard)
 3154:     croak if column name is not in _col_db (unknown column)
 3155:     if join_map or filters are active:
 3156:         parse calling arguments using _parse_query_args
 3157:         call _joined_query to get all merged rows
 3158:         return map { $_->{col} } @rows  in list context
 3159:         return $rows[0]{col}            in scalar context
 3160:     else:
 3161:         delegate directly to the owning database
 3162: 
 3163: =head3 MESSAGES
 3164: 
 3165:     (croak) Database::Join: cannot call private method '_NAME' via AUTOLOAD
 3166:         -- Method name begins with '_'.  Private methods must be called directly,
 3167:            not via AUTOLOAD.  This is a programming error.
 3168: 
 3169:     (croak) Database::Join: unknown column 'NAME'
 3170:         -- Method name does not match any visible column in the merged view.
 3171:            Check spelling, or whether the column was removed with remove_column().
 3172: 
 3173: =cut
 3174: 
 3175: our $AUTOLOAD;
 3176:

 3565: # _joined_query_array( \%params ) -> \@merged_rows
 3566: #
 3567: # Purpose: Core in-memory join algorithm.  Partitions criteria, fetches per-database
 3568: #          results, resolves the key set, and merges rows.
 3569: #
 3570: # Key-set resolution (applied for each secondary database after the primary):
 3571: #
 3572: #   If the database had criteria in this query call (after base filter overlay),
 3573: #   it acts as an INNER-JOIN partner: only keys present in its filtered result
 3574: #   survive.  This gives WHERE-clause semantics even under a LEFT join.
 3575: #
 3576: #   If the database had NO effective criteria:
 3577: #     inner  -> intersect  (standard inner join)
 3578: #     left   -> no change  (primary defines the key set)
 3579: #     outer  -> union      (all keys from any database)
 3580: #
 3581: # Row merge: for each qualifying primary row, secondary rows are overlaid in
 3582: # index order.  For duplicate columns, later databases win.  Local join-key
 3583: # aliases are renamed to the canonical join_column before merging.
 3584: # Removed columns are deleted from every merged row.

 5355: # _copy_criteria( \%criteria ) -> \%copy
 5356: # Purpose: Return a two-level deep copy of a single criteria hashref so that
 5357: #          post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot change the
 5358: #          stored filter.  Operator sub-hashrefs (e.g. { '>' => 80 }) are
 5359: #          shallow-copied one additional level, matching the broadcast-copy
 5360: #          idiom used in _partition_criteria for join-column criteria.
 5361: # Entry:   $criteria is a hashref (may be undef).
 5362: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.
 5363: sub _copy_criteria {
 5364: 	my ($criteria) = @_;
 5365: 	return {} unless $criteria && %{$criteria};
 5366: 	return {
 5367: 		map {
 5368: 			$_ => ref($criteria->{$_}) eq 'HASH'
 5369: 				? { %{ $criteria->{$_} } }  # shallow-copy operator sub-hashref
 5370: 				: $criteria->{$_}
 5371: 		} keys %{$criteria}
 5372: 	};
 5373: }
 5374: 
 5375: # _copy_filters( \%filters ) -> \%copy
 5376: # Purpose: Deep-copy the filters hashref (db_index => criteria_hashref) so
 5377: #          that post-construction mutation of the caller's hash cannot silently
 5378: #          bypass the inner-join row-security guarantee.
 5379: # Entry:   $filters may be undef.
 5380: # Exit:    Returns a new hashref; never returns the input reference itself.
 5381: sub _copy_filters {
 5382: 	my ($filters) = @_;

 5384: 	return { map { $_ => _copy_criteria($filters->{$_}) } keys %{$filters} };
 5385: }
